## Comprehensive Skills Exploration Report

I have thoroughly explored the skills structure in this recommendations-service project. Here's what I found:

### Skills Directory Structure

## Skill Inventory & Structures

### 1. **starting-jira-tasks** 
**Location:** `.claude/skills/starting-jira-tasks/`
**Files:** SKILL.md, baseline-results.md, test-scenarios.md
**Format:** Core SKILL.md with reference documentation
**Purpose:** Automated Jira task workflow - fetches task status, conditionally transitions to "In Progress" (only if status is "To Do"), then manages git branch checkout/creation
**Key Pattern:** Conditional Jira API transitions + git workflow with strict one-line output format
**No /loop equivalent** - this is a complete standalone workflow

### 2. **create-pr**
**Location:** `.claude/skills/create-pr/`
**Files:** SKILL.md only
**Format:** Single comprehensive file with 10 sequential steps
**Purpose:** Automated PR creation with Jira task validation
**Key Workflow:** Detect base branch (critical for stacked branches), extract task ID, fetch Jira details, analyze committed changes, identify gaps, request confirmation, generate PR body using template, create draft PR
**Critical Pattern:** Stacked branch detection (branches cut from previous task branches, not main)

### 3. **verify**

- E2E tests in `/test/*.e2e-spec.ts` (collects coverage only from `*.(controller|service).ts`)
- Mock patterns, async testing, HTTP endpoint testing, database testing patterns
- 95% coverage minimum requirement

- Always use knexRead for SELECT, knexWrite for INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
- Standard schema pattern: `_id` (UUID PK), `created_at`, `updated_at`, `deleted_at` (soft delete)
**Patterns Covered:**

- Model CRUD patterns with validation

├── scripts/ (optional, executable code)

## Skill Format Analysis

### YAML Frontmatter (Required in all SKILL.md)
```yaml
---
name: skill-name
description: Clear description of what skill does and when to use it
[license: optional]
[metadata: optional]
---
```

### Progressive Disclosure Pattern
1. **Metadata (~100 words)** - name + description, always in context
2. **SKILL.md body (<5k words)** - Loaded when skill triggers
3. **Bundled resources** - Loaded as needed:
   - `scripts/` - Executable code (deterministic reliability)
   - `references/` - Documentation (loaded as-needed)
   - `assets/` - Templates, boilerplate, images

### File Organization Best Practices
- NO README.md, INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md, CHANGELOG.md (extraneous files)
- Only include essential files that directly support functionality
- Reference files should be one level deep from SKILL.md
- Longer reference files (>100 lines) should include table of contents

---

## Key Observations

1. **No /loop skill exists** - The project doesn't implement a loop/iteration skill. Workflows are sequential and deterministic.

2. **Stacked Branch Philosophy** - The `create-pr` skill reveals this project uses stacked branches where each branch is cut from the previous task's branch, not from main. This is critical context for PR creation.

3. **Safety-First Migrations** - The `database-skill` emphasizes that destructive operations (DROP, DELETE, TRUNCATE) must ONLY be in migrations, never executed directly. This is a critical safety rule.

4. **Modular Organization** - Larger skills like `react-best-practices` and `skill-creator` demonstrate how to split content into separate reference files to keep SKILL.md lean while maintaining discoverable organization.

5. **Frontmatter is Triggering Mechanism** - The `description` field in YAML frontmatter is the PRIMARY trigger for when Claude should use the skill. It includes both what the skill does and specific triggers/contexts.

6. **Explicit Output Formats** - Skills like `starting-jira-tasks` and `load-test-report` specify exact output formats (e.g., one-line format, structured tables) to ensure consistent, predictable behavior.

7. **Skill Orchestration** - Some skills reference and trigger other skills (e.g., `documentation-skill` references `diagram-skill` for ASCII diagrams; `nestjs-skill` mentions orchestrating `code-quality-skill` and `test-skill`).

8. **Test Files in Structure** - The `starting-jira-tasks` skill includes supplementary test files (`baseline-results.md`, `test-scenarios.md`) for validation, though these are optional.

## Summary: /loop, /schedule Skills and Automation Tools

Based on my comprehensive search of the Claude Code ecosystem, here's what I found regarding the /loop skill, /schedule skill, CronCreate/CronList tools, RemoteTrigger tool, and automation patterns:

### 1. **CronCreate and CronList Tools (Session-Scoped Scheduling)**

**Documentation:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks

The CronCreate and CronList tools power Claude Code's session-scoped task scheduling:

- **CronCreate**: Accepts a 5-field cron expression, a prompt to run, and whether it recurs or fires once
- **CronList**: Lists all scheduled tasks with their IDs, schedules, and prompts  
- **CronDelete**: Cancel a task by ID

**Key constraints:**
- Up to 50 scheduled tasks per session maximum
- Recurring tasks auto-expire after 3 days (fire once more, then delete)
- All times interpreted in local timezone (not UTC)
- Jitter: recurring tasks fire up to 10% of period late (max 15 min); one-shot tasks on :00/:30 fire up to 90 seconds early
- Only fire while Claude Code is running and idle
- Session-scoped (vanish on restart)

**Cron expression format:** `minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week`
- Supports: wildcards (*), single values (5), steps (*/15), ranges (1-5), lists (1,15,30)
- Day-of-week: 0 or 7 = Sunday, 1-6 = Mon-Sat
- No extended syntax (L, W, ?, name aliases like MON/JAN)

### 2. **The /loop Bundled Skill**

**Documentation:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks

/loop is a built-in Claude Code skill (pre-installed) that makes scheduling easy:

**Usage patterns:**
```
/loop 5m check if the deployment finished and tell me what happened
/loop 30m check the build
/loop check the build every 2 hours
/loop 20m /review-pr 1234  # Loop over another command/skill
/loop check the build      # Defaults to every 10 minutes
```

**What it does:**
- Parses natural language intervals (5m, 30m, 2h, 1d)
- Converts to cron expressions
- Schedules recurring jobs
- Confirms cadence and job ID

**Interval support:** seconds (s), minutes (m), hours (h), days (d)
- Seconds rounded up to nearest minute (cron granularity)
- Non-even intervals (7m, 90m) rounded to nearest clean interval

### 3. **One-Time Reminders (Non-/loop)**

**Documentation:** https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks

Natural language reminders that fire once:
```
remind me at 3pm to push the release branch
in 45 minutes, check whether the integration tests passed
```

Claude pins fire time to specific minute/hour using cron and confirms when it fires.

### 4. **RemoteTrigger Tool**

**Status:** This tool provides programmatic access to the cloud scheduled task REST API

**Key endpoints (via RemoteTrigger tool):**
- **list**: GET `/v1/code/triggers` - List all cloud scheduled tasks
- **get**: GET `/v1/code/triggers/{trigger_id}` - Get specific task details
- **create**: POST `/v1/code/triggers` - Create new cloud scheduled task
- **update**: POST `/v1/code/triggers/{trigger_id}` - Modify existing task
- **run**: POST `/v1/code/triggers/{trigger_id}/run` - Execute immediately

**Usage in Claude Code:**
```typescript
RemoteTrigger({ action: "list" })           // See all tasks
RemoteTrigger({ action: "get", trigger_id: "xyz" })
RemoteTrigger({ action: "create", body: {...} })  // Create new
RemoteTrigger({ action: "update", trigger_id: "xyz", body: {...} })
RemoteTrigger({ action: "run", trigger_id: "xyz" })
```

**Important:** RemoteTrigger provides OAuth token automatically (no manual token handling needed)

### 5. **Cloud vs Desktop vs /loop Scheduling Comparison**

| Feature | Cloud | Desktop | /loop |
|---------|-------|---------|-------|
| Runs on | Anthropic cloud | Your machine | Your machine |
| Machine must stay on | No | Yes | Yes |
| Requires open session | No | No | **Yes** |
| Persistent on restart | Yes | Yes | No |
| Local file access | No (fresh clone) | Yes | Yes |
| MCP servers | Per-task connectors | Config + connectors | Inherits from session |
| Minimum interval | 1 hour | 1 minute | 1 minute |
| Customizable schedule | Via `/schedule` CLI | Yes | Yes |

### 6. **Existing Automation Patterns in the Codebase**

**Hooks patterns** (from `/Users/4thwithme/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/claude-code-setup/1.0.0/skills/claude-automation-recommender/references/hooks-patterns.md`):

- **PostToolUse hooks:** Auto-format, auto-lint, type-check, run tests on file edits
- **PreToolUse hooks:** Block sensitive files (.env, secrets), lock files

     1→---
     2→name: starting-jira-tasks
     3→description: Use when user says "start task RECO-XXX" or "start RECO-XXX" or similar phrasing to begin work on a Jira task
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Starting Jira Tasks
     7→
     8→## Overview
     9→
    10→"Start task RECO-XXX" means: checkout or create git branch + conditionally transition Jira status to "In Progress" (only if current status is "To Do"). If a branch starting with RECO-XXX already exists, checkout that branch. Otherwise, create a new one.
    11→
    12→## The Complete Workflow
    13→
    14→```dot
    15→digraph start_task {
    16→    "User: start task RECO-XXX" [shape=doublecircle];
    17→    "Infer full task key" [shape=box];
    18→    "Fetch current task status" [shape=box];
    19→    "Is status 'To Do'?" [shape=diamond];
    20→    "Get available transitions" [shape=box];
    21→    "Find 'In Progress' transition" [shape=diamond];
    22→    "Transition to In Progress" [shape=box];
    23→    "Git: stash if needed" [shape=box];
    24→    "Git: checkout main" [shape=box];
    25→    "Git: pull latest" [shape=box];
    26→    "Check for existing RECO-XXX* branch" [shape=box];
    27→    "Branch exists?" [shape=diamond];
    28→    "Git: checkout existing branch" [shape=box];
    29→    "Git: create new branch RECO-XXX*" [shape=box];
    30→    "Done - silent" [shape=doublecircle];
    31→    "Error: No transition available" [shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=red];
    32→
    33→    "User: start task RECO-XXX" -> "Infer full task key";
    34→    "Infer full task key" -> "Fetch current task status";
    35→    "Fetch current task status" -> "Is status 'To Do'?";
    36→    "Is status 'To Do'?" -> "Get available transitions" [label="yes"];
    37→    "Is status 'To Do'?" -> "Git: stash if needed" [label="no"];
    38→    "Get available transitions" -> "Find 'In Progress' transition";
    39→    "Find 'In Progress' transition" -> "Transition to In Progress" [label="found"];
    40→    "Find 'In Progress' transition" -> "Error: No transition available" [label="not found"];
    41→    "Transition to In Progress" -> "Git: stash if needed";
    42→    "Git: stash if needed" -> "Git: checkout main";
    43→    "Git: checkout main" -> "Git: pull latest";
    44→    "Git: pull latest" -> "Check for existing RECO-XXX* branch";
    45→    "Check for existing RECO-XXX* branch" -> "Branch exists?";
    46→    "Branch exists?" -> "Git: checkout existing branch" [label="yes"];
    47→    "Branch exists?" -> "Git: create new branch RECO-XXX*" [label="no"];
    48→    "Git: checkout existing branch" -> "Done - silent";
    49→    "Git: create new branch RECO-XXX*" -> "Done - silent";
    50→}
    51→```
    52→
    53→## Quick Reference
    54→
    55→| User Input | Inferred Task Key |
    56→|------------|-------------------|
    57→| "start task RECO-524" | RECO-524 |
    58→| "start RECO-349" | RECO-349 |
    59→| "start 524" | RECO-524 |
    60→| "begin work on 349" | RECO-349 |
    61→
    62→## Implementation Steps
    63→
    64→### 1. Get Atlassian Cloud ID
    65→
    66→Read cloud ID from environment file:
    67→
    68→```bash
    69→grep ATLASSIAN_CLOUD_ID .env | cut -d'=' -f2
    70→```
    71→
    72→Store for use in Jira API calls.
    73→
    74→### 2. Infer Full Task Key
    75→If user provides just number (e.g., "349"), prepend "RECO-" to get "RECO-349".
    76→
    77→### 3. Transition Jira Status (CONDITIONAL)
    78→
    79→**Only transition if current status is "To Do".**
    80→
    81→Use cloud ID from step 1:
    82→
    83→```typescript
    84→// First, fetch current task status
    85→const task = await getJiraIssue({
    86→  cloudId: CLOUD_ID_FROM_STEP_1,
    87→  issueIdOrKey: taskKey
    88→});
    89→
    90→// Only transition if status is "To Do"
    91→if (task.fields.status.name === "To Do") {
    92→  // Get available transitions
    93→  const transitions = await getTransitionsForJiraIssue({
    94→    cloudId: CLOUD_ID_FROM_STEP_1,
    95→    issueIdOrKey: taskKey
    96→  });
    97→
    98→  // Find "In Progress" transition
    99→  const inProgressTransition = transitions.find(t =>
   100→    t.name === "In Progress"
   101→  );
   102→
   103→  if (!inProgressTransition) {
   104→    throw new Error(`No "In Progress" transition available for ${taskKey}`);
   105→  }
   106→
   107→  // Execute transition
   108→  await transitionJiraIssue({
   109→    cloudId: CLOUD_ID_FROM_STEP_1,
   110→    issueIdOrKey: taskKey,
   111→    transition: { id: inProgressTransition.id }
   112→  });
   113→}
   114→// If status is NOT "To Do", skip transition and proceed to git workflow
   115→```
   116→
   117→### 4. Git Workflow (REQUIRED)
   118→
   119→```bash
   120→# Stash uncommitted work if present (only if there are changes)
   121→if [ -n "$(git status --porcelain)" ]; then
   122→  git stash
   123→fi
   124→
   125→# Get latest from main
   126→git checkout main
   127→git pull
   128→
   129→# Check if branch starting with RECO-XXX already exists (exact match or with suffix)
   130→# This finds: RECO-XXX, RECO-XXX-fix, RECO-XXX-anything
   131→existing_branch=$(git branch --list "RECO-XXX*" | head -1 | sed 's/^[ *]*//')
   132→
   133→if [ -n "$existing_branch" ]; then
   134→  # Branch exists - checkout existing branch
   135→  git checkout "$existing_branch"
   136→else
   137→  # Branch doesn't exist - create new branch
   138→  git checkout -b RECO-XXX-descriptive-name
   139→fi
   140→```
   141→
   142→**Branch naming:** `RECO-XXX` or `RECO-XXX-descriptive-name` where descriptive-name is kebab-case summary.
   143→
   144→**Important:** Only create a new branch if no branch starting with `RECO-XXX` exists. If one exists, checkout that existing branch. The search pattern `RECO-XXX*` (without hyphen after XXX) finds both exact matches (`RECO-XXX`) and branches with suffixes (`RECO-XXX-fix-bug`).
   145→
   146→### 5. Silent Completion
   147→
   148→**EXACT OUTPUT FORMAT:**
   149→```
   150→Started RECO-XXX on branch RECO-XXX-branch-name
   151→```
   152→
   153→**FORBIDDEN OUTPUT:**
   154→- ❌ Task summary/description
   155→- ❌ "The bug involves..." or any task explanation
   156→- ❌ "next steps" or "ready to..."
   157→- ❌ "would you like me to..."
   158→- ❌ Multi-paragraph responses
   159→- ❌ Headers like "## Status Report" or "**Task summary:**"
   160→
   161→**If you add ANYTHING beyond the one-line format above, you are violating this skill.**
   162→
   163→## Common Mistakes
   164→
   165→| Mistake | Fix |
   166→|---------|-----|
   167→| Transition status when NOT "To Do" | Check current status first. Only transition from "To Do" to "In Progress". |
   168→| Create new branch when one exists | Check for existing RECO-XXX* branch first (matches RECO-XXX and RECO-XXX-suffix). Checkout if exists, create if not. |
   169→| Claim status changed without API call | Never lie. Use transitionJiraIssue API when needed. |
   170→| Provide task summary | User already knows the task. Just start it. |
   171→| Ask clarifying questions | User said "start task" - that's clear instruction. |
   172→| Skip git workflow | Git workflow is ALWAYS required. |
   173→| Wrong branch name | Must start with RECO-XXX. |
   174→
   175→## Red Flags - STOP
   176→
   177→These thoughts mean you're about to violate the skill:
   178→
   179→- "Let me get task details to help you start" → NO. Start means transition + branch.
   180→- "I'll fetch the task so you can begin" → NO. Beginning means executing the workflow.
   181→- "Should I transition the status?" → Check current status. Only if "To Do".
   182→- "Do you want me to create a branch?" → Check if RECO-XXX branch exists first. Checkout or create.
   183→- "I should explain what this task is about" → NO. One line output only.
   184→- "The user might want context about the bug" → NO. They asked to START, not explain.
   185→- Writing more than one line of output → STOP. You're violating silent completion.
   186→
   187→## Rationalizations That Are Wrong
   188→
   189→| Excuse | Reality |
   190→|--------|---------|
   191→| "User just wants task details" | "Start task" means conditional transition + branch. Details are incidental. |
   192→| "I'll help them prepare to start" | Starting = doing the workflow, not preparing. |
   193→| "Status transition isn't part of git workflow" | Conditional transition + git workflow both required for "start task". |
   194→| "I should ask what branch name they want" | Use convention: RECO-XXX-descriptive-name. |
   195→| "Let me check if they meant something else" | "Start task RECO-XXX" is unambiguous. |
   196→| "I should transition regardless of current status" | NO. Only transition from "To Do" to "In Progress". |
   197→| "I'll create a new branch with a better name" | NO. Check for existing RECO-XXX branch first. Use existing if found. |
   198→| "The existing branch might be old/wrong" | NO. If RECO-XXX branch exists, use it. Don't create duplicates. |
   199→
   200→## When NOT to Use This Skill
   201→
   202→- User asks "what is RECO-XXX about?" → Fetch task details only
   203→- User asks "show me RECO-XXX" → Fetch task details only
   204→- User asks "create branch for RECO-XXX" → Git only, no Jira transition
   205→- User asks "move RECO-XXX to in progress" → Jira transition only, no git
   206→
   207→Only use complete workflow when user says "start task" or equivalent phrasing indicating beginning work.
```

```
     1→---
     2→name: create-pr
     3→description: Create pull requests with automated validation against Jira tasks. Use when user requests to create a PR, make a pull request, or open a pull request. Validates committed changes against Jira task description, identifies missing work, gets user confirmation, and creates draft PR with fulfilled template.
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Create PR
     7→
     8→Automated pull request creation workflow with Jira task validation.
     9→
    10→## Workflow
    11→
    12→Follow these steps sequentially:
    13→
    14→### 1. Get Atlassian Cloud ID
    15→
    16→Read cloud ID from environment file:
    17→
    18→```bash
    19→grep ATLASSIAN_CLOUD_ID .env | cut -d'=' -f2
    20→```
    21→
    22→Store for use in Jira API calls.
    23→
    24→### 2. Detect Base Branch (CRITICAL)
    25→
    26→Branches in this repo are stacked — each branch is cut from the previous task's branch, not from `main`. Using `main` as base would include all upstream commits in the diff, polluting the PR.
    27→
    28→Detect the correct base branch:
    29→
    30→```bash
    31→# Show current branch
    32→git branch --show-current
    33→
    34→# Find the most recent remote branch this branch was cut from
    35→# (excludes HEAD and the current branch itself)
    36→git log --format='%D' HEAD | grep 'origin/' | grep -v 'HEAD' | head -5
    37→
    38→# Alternative: find remote branches containing the fork point
    39→git branch -r --contains $(git merge-base HEAD origin/main) | grep -v "$(git branch --show-current)" | head -10
    40→```
    41→
    42→**Rules:**
    43→- If the branch was cut directly from `main` → base = `main`
    44→- If the branch was cut from another feature branch → base = that feature branch (e.g., `RECO-646-fix-eslint-root-categories-style-alternatives`)
    45→- When uncertain → ask the user: "What branch was this cut from?"
    46→
    47→Store the base branch name. Use it for ALL subsequent `git log` and `git diff` commands and for `--base` in `gh pr create`.
    48→
    49→### 3. Validate Branch State
    50→
    51→Check current branch and commit status against the **detected base branch**:
    52→
    53→```bash
    54→git log origin/<BASE_BRANCH>..HEAD --oneline
    55→```
    56→
    57→**Exit conditions:**
    58→
    59→- Current branch is `main` or `master` → Respond: "No changes"
    60→- No commits ahead of base branch → Respond: "No changes"
    61→
    62→### 4. Extract Jira Task ID
    63→
    64→Parse task ID from branch name using pattern `RECO-\d+`:
    65→
    66→**Examples:**
    67→
    68→- `RECO-349-task-naming` → Task ID: `RECO-349`
    69→- `RECO-1234-fix-bug` → Task ID: `RECO-1234`
    70→
    71→If no match found → Ask user: "Cannot extract task ID from branch name. Provide task ID or continue without validation?"
    72→
    73→### 5. Fetch Jira Task
    74→
    75→Use Atlassian MCP tool with cloud ID from step 1:
    76→
    77→```typescript
    78→mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__getJiraIssue({
    79→	cloudId: CLOUD_ID_FROM_STEP_1,
    80→	issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-349',
    81→});
    82→```
    83→
    84→Extract:
    85→
    86→- Task title
    87→- Task description
    88→- Acceptance criteria
    89→- Subtasks (if any)
    90→
    91→### 6. Analyze Committed Changes
    92→
    93→Review all commits in current branch against the base branch:
    94→
    95→```bash
    96→git log origin/<BASE_BRANCH>..HEAD --stat
    97→git diff origin/<BASE_BRANCH>..HEAD
    98→```
    99→
   100→Read every changed file to understand what was modified and why. Group changes by concern:
   101→
   102→- New files created
   103→- Existing files modified (what logic changed and why)
   104→- Config/infra changes
   105→- Test changes
   106→
   107→Compare changes against:
   108→
   109→- Task description requirements
   110→- Acceptance criteria
   111→
   112→### 7. Identify Gaps
   113→
   114→Create list of missing items:
   115→
   116→- Uncompleted requirements from task description
   117→- Missing acceptance criteria implementations
   118→- Missing tests (if specified)
   119→
   120→**Format:**
   121→
   122→```
   123→Missing items:
   124→- [ ] Item 1 description
   125→- [ ] Item 2 description
   126→- [ ] Item 3 description
   127→```
   128→
   129→If no gaps found → State: "All task requirements appear to be implemented."
   130→
   131→### 8. Request Confirmation
   132→
   133→Present findings and ask for confirmation:
   134→
   135→```
   136→Summary:
   137→- Branch: [branch-name]
   138→- Base: [base-branch]
   139→- Task: [TASK-ID] - [task title]
   140→- Commits: [count] commits
   141→
   142→[Missing items list or "All requirements met"]
   143→
   144→Create draft PR with current state?
   145→```
   146→
   147→Wait for user confirmation. If declined → Stop.
   148→
   149→### 9. Generate PR Body
   150→
   151→Read the PR template:
   152→
   153→```bash
   154→cat .github/pull_request_template.md
   155→```
   156→
   157→Fill every section with **specific, detailed content** — not placeholders:
   158→
   159→#### Ticket section
   160→```
   161→JIRA task: [RECO-XXX](https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX)
   162→Design: [Figma Link](url) — omit if no Figma link
   163→```
   164→
   165→#### Changes section
   166→List each changed file or group of related files with a precise explanation:
   167→
   168→```markdown
   169→- **`path/to/file.ts`** — What changed and why (e.g., "Added X to fix Y", "Removed Z because it was replaced by W")
   170→- **`path/to/other.ts`** — What changed and why
   171→- **`path/to/test.unit-spec.ts`** — What test cases were added/modified and what behavior they cover
   172→```
   173→
   174→Be specific: mention function names, config keys, hook types, or algorithm changes. Do not write vague summaries like "updated service" or "fixed bug".
   175→
   176→#### PR Checks section
   177→Check each box that applies based on the actual committed changes:
   178→
   179→```markdown
   180→- [x] I've self-reviewed my code.
   181→- [x] I've added the necessary labels.
   182→- [x] I've added tests for my code.        ← only if tests exist in the diff
   183→- [x] I've updated the documentation.     ← only if docs were updated
   184→```
   185→
   186→#### Description / Ticket Summary section
   187→Write a concise paragraph (3-5 sentences) explaining:
   188→- What the feature/fix does at a business/product level
   189→- Why it was needed
   190→- Any notable design decisions or trade-offs
   191→
   192→Copy from the Jira ticket description if it's good; rewrite if it's vague.
   193→
   194→#### Screenshots / Short Videos
   195→Omit if not applicable (backend-only changes). Add if UI changes.
   196→
   197→### 10. Create Draft PR
   198→
   199→```bash
   200→gh pr create \
   201→  --draft \
   202→  --base <BASE_BRANCH> \
   203→  --title "RECO-XXX: [task title from Jira]" \
   204→  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
   205→[Filled template content — all sections populated with specific content]
   206→EOF
   207→)"
   208→```
   209→
   210→Respond with PR URL and summary.
   211→
   212→## Notes
   213→
   214→- Always create as **draft** PR
   215→- Always pass `--base <BASE_BRANCH>` — never let `gh` default to `main` when the branch is stacked
   216→- Task ID pattern: `RECO-\d+` (project-specific)
   217→- Use existing tools: `gh` CLI, Atlassian MCP, Git
   218→- Validation is advisory, not blocking — user decides whether to proceed
   219→- PR body must have real content in every section — no template placeholders left unfilled
   220→
```

     1→---
     2→name: verify
     3→description: Pre-PR verification — runs the full quality suite (lint, type-check, unit tests, e2e tests) and confirms all checks pass before declaring work complete. Use before creating a PR or claiming a task is done.
     4→---
     5→
     6→Run the full verification suite in order. Stop at the first failure and fix it before continuing.
     7→
     8→## Steps
     9→
    10→1. **Code quality** (fast — run first):
    11→   ```bash
    12→   npm run code-quality-check
    13→   ```
    14→   Fix any lint, format, or type-check errors before proceeding.
    15→
    16→2. **Unit tests**:
    17→   ```bash
    18→   npm run test:unit
    19→   ```
    20→   All tests must pass. If coverage fails, fix the gaps.
    21→
    22→3. **E2E tests**:
    23→   ```bash
    24→   npm run test:e2e
    25→   ```
    26→   E2E tests auto-reset the test DB. All must pass.
    27→
    28→## Rules
    29→
    30→- Do NOT claim work is complete or create a PR until all three steps pass.
    31→- If any step fails, fix the issue, then re-run that step (not the full suite) to confirm the fix.
    32→- Report results: "All checks pass: lint ✓, unit ✓, e2e ✓" or list what failed.
    33→- Coverage threshold is 95% across branches/functions/lines/statements — failing coverage is a failing check.
    34→
```

     1→---
     2→name: skill-creator
     3→description: Guide for creating effective skills. This skill should be used when users want to create a new skill (or update an existing skill) that extends Claude's capabilities with specialized knowledge, workflows, or tool integrations.
     4→license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt
     5→---
     6→
     7→# Skill Creator
     8→
     9→This skill provides guidance for creating effective skills.
    10→
    11→## About Skills
    12→
    13→Skills are modular, self-contained packages that extend Claude's capabilities by providing
    14→specialized knowledge, workflows, and tools. Think of them as "onboarding guides" for specific
    15→domains or tasks—they transform Claude from a general-purpose agent into a specialized agent
    16→equipped with procedural knowledge that no model can fully possess.
    17→
    18→### What Skills Provide
    19→
    20→1. Specialized workflows - Multi-step procedures for specific domains
    21→2. Tool integrations - Instructions for working with specific file formats or APIs
    22→3. Domain expertise - Company-specific knowledge, schemas, business logic
    23→4. Bundled resources - Scripts, references, and assets for complex and repetitive tasks
    24→
    25→## Core Principles
    26→
    27→### Concise is Key
    28→
    29→The context window is a public good. Skills share the context window with everything else Claude needs: system prompt, conversation history, other Skills' metadata, and the actual user request.
    30→
    31→**Default assumption: Claude is already very smart.** Only add context Claude doesn't already have. Challenge each piece of information: "Does Claude really need this explanation?" and "Does this paragraph justify its token cost?"
    32→
    33→Prefer concise examples over verbose explanations.
    34→
    35→### Set Appropriate Degrees of Freedom
    36→
    37→Match the level of specificity to the task's fragility and variability:
    38→
    39→**High freedom (text-based instructions)**: Use when multiple approaches are valid, decisions depend on context, or heuristics guide the approach.
    40→
    41→**Medium freedom (pseudocode or scripts with parameters)**: Use when a preferred pattern exists, some variation is acceptable, or configuration affects behavior.
    42→
    43→**Low freedom (specific scripts, few parameters)**: Use when operations are fragile and error-prone, consistency is critical, or a specific sequence must be followed.
    44→
    45→Think of Claude as exploring a path: a narrow bridge with cliffs needs specific guardrails (low freedom), while an open field allows many routes (high freedom).
    46→
    47→### Anatomy of a Skill
    48→
    49→Every skill consists of a required SKILL.md file and optional bundled resources:
    50→
    51→```
    52→skill-name/
    53→├── SKILL.md (required)
    54→│   ├── YAML frontmatter metadata (required)
    55→│   │   ├── name: (required)
    56→│   │   └── description: (required)
    57→│   └── Markdown instructions (required)
    58→└── Bundled Resources (optional)
    59→    ├── scripts/          - Executable code (Python/Bash/etc.)
    60→    ├── references/       - Documentation intended to be loaded into context as needed
    61→    └── assets/           - Files used in output (templates, icons, fonts, etc.)
    62→```
    63→
    64→#### SKILL.md (required)
    65→
    66→Every SKILL.md consists of:
    67→
    68→- **Frontmatter** (YAML): Contains `name` and `description` fields. These are the only fields that Claude reads to determine when the skill gets used, thus it is very important to be clear and comprehensive in describing what the skill is, and when it should be used.
    69→- **Body** (Markdown): Instructions and guidance for using the skill. Only loaded AFTER the skill triggers (if at all).
    70→
    71→#### Bundled Resources (optional)
    72→
    73→##### Scripts (`scripts/`)
    74→
    75→Executable code (Python/Bash/etc.) for tasks that require deterministic reliability or are repeatedly rewritten.
    76→
    77→- **When to include**: When the same code is being rewritten repeatedly or deterministic reliability is needed
    78→- **Example**: `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` for PDF rotation tasks
    79→- **Benefits**: Token efficient, deterministic, may be executed without loading into context
    80→- **Note**: Scripts may still need to be read by Claude for patching or environment-specific adjustments
    81→
    82→##### References (`references/`)
    83→
    84→Documentation and reference material intended to be loaded as needed into context to inform Claude's process and thinking.
    85→
    86→- **When to include**: For documentation that Claude should reference while working
    87→- **Examples**: `references/finance.md` for financial schemas, `references/mnda.md` for company NDA template, `references/policies.md` for company policies, `references/api_docs.md` for API specifications
    88→- **Use cases**: Database schemas, API documentation, domain knowledge, company policies, detailed workflow guides
    89→- **Benefits**: Keeps SKILL.md lean, loaded only when Claude determines it's needed
    90→- **Best practice**: If files are large (>10k words), include grep search patterns in SKILL.md
    91→- **Avoid duplication**: Information should live in either SKILL.md or references files, not both. Prefer references files for detailed information unless it's truly core to the skill—this keeps SKILL.md lean while making information discoverable without hogging the context window. Keep only essential procedural instructions and workflow guidance in SKILL.md; move detailed reference material, schemas, and examples to references files.
    92→
    93→##### Assets (`assets/`)
    94→
    95→Files not intended to be loaded into context, but rather used within the output Claude produces.
    96→
    97→- **When to include**: When the skill needs files that will be used in the final output
    98→- **Examples**: `assets/logo.png` for brand assets, `assets/slides.pptx` for PowerPoint templates, `assets/frontend-template/` for HTML/React boilerplate, `assets/font.ttf` for typography
    99→- **Use cases**: Templates, images, icons, boilerplate code, fonts, sample documents that get copied or modified
   100→- **Benefits**: Separates output resources from documentation, enables Claude to use files without loading them into context
   101→
   102→#### What to Not Include in a Skill
   103→
   104→A skill should only contain essential files that directly support its functionality. Do NOT create extraneous documentation or auxiliary files, including:
   105→
   106→- README.md
   107→- INSTALLATION_GUIDE.md
   108→- QUICK_REFERENCE.md
   109→- CHANGELOG.md
   110→- etc.
   111→
   112→The skill should only contain the information needed for an AI agent to do the job at hand. It should not contain auxilary context about the process that went into creating it, setup and testing procedures, user-facing documentation, etc. Creating additional documentation files just adds clutter and confusion.
   113→
   114→### Progressive Disclosure Design Principle
   115→
   116→Skills use a three-level loading system to manage context efficiently:
   117→
   118→1. **Metadata (name + description)** - Always in context (~100 words)
   119→2. **SKILL.md body** - When skill triggers (<5k words)
   120→3. **Bundled resources** - As needed by Claude (Unlimited because scripts can be executed without reading into context window)
   121→
   122→#### Progressive Disclosure Patterns
   123→
   124→Keep SKILL.md body to the essentials and under 500 lines to minimize context bloat. Split content into separate files when approaching this limit. When splitting out content into other files, it is very important to reference them from SKILL.md and describe clearly when to read them, to ensure the reader of the skill knows they exist and when to use them.
   125→
   126→**Key principle:** When a skill supports multiple variations, frameworks, or options, keep only the core workflow and selection guidance in SKILL.md. Move variant-specific details (patterns, examples, configuration) into separate reference files.
   127→
   128→**Pattern 1: High-level guide with references**
   129→
   130→```markdown
   131→# PDF Processing
   132→
   133→## Quick start
   134→
   135→Extract text with pdfplumber:
   136→[code example]
   137→
   138→## Advanced features
   139→
   140→- **Form filling**: See [FORMS.md](FORMS.md) for complete guide
   141→- **API reference**: See [REFERENCE.md](REFERENCE.md) for all methods
   142→- **Examples**: See [EXAMPLES.md](EXAMPLES.md) for common patterns
   143→```
   144→
   145→Claude loads FORMS.md, REFERENCE.md, or EXAMPLES.md only when needed.
   146→
   147→**Pattern 2: Domain-specific organization**
   148→
   149→For Skills with multiple domains, organize content by domain to avoid loading irrelevant context:
   150→
   151→```
   152→bigquery-skill/
   153→├── SKILL.md (overview and navigation)
   154→└── reference/
   155→    ├── finance.md (revenue, billing metrics)
   156→    ├── sales.md (opportunities, pipeline)
   157→    ├── product.md (API usage, features)
   158→    └── marketing.md (campaigns, attribution)
   159→```
   160→
   161→When a user asks about sales metrics, Claude only reads sales.md.
   162→
   163→Similarly, for skills supporting multiple frameworks or variants, organize by variant:
   164→
   165→```
   166→cloud-deploy/
   167→├── SKILL.md (workflow + provider selection)
   168→└── references/
   169→    ├── aws.md (AWS deployment patterns)
   170→    ├── gcp.md (GCP deployment patterns)
   171→    └── azure.md (Azure deployment patterns)
   172→```
   173→
   174→When the user chooses AWS, Claude only reads aws.md.
   175→
   176→**Pattern 3: Conditional details**
   177→
   178→Show basic content, link to advanced content:
   179→
   180→```markdown
   181→# DOCX Processing
   182→
   183→## Creating documents
   184→
   185→Use docx-js for new documents. See [DOCX-JS.md](DOCX-JS.md).
   186→
   187→## Editing documents
   188→
   189→For simple edits, modify the XML directly.
   190→
   191→**For tracked changes**: See [REDLINING.md](REDLINING.md)
   192→**For OOXML details**: See [OOXML.md](OOXML.md)
   193→```
   194→
   195→Claude reads REDLINING.md or OOXML.md only when the user needs those features.
   196→
   197→**Important guidelines:**
   198→
   199→- **Avoid deeply nested references** - Keep references one level deep from SKILL.md. All reference files should link directly from SKILL.md.
   200→- **Structure longer reference files** - For files longer than 100 lines, include a table of contents at the top so Claude can see the full scope when previewing.
   201→
   202→## Skill Creation Process
   203→
   204→Skill creation involves these steps:
   205→
   206→1. Understand the skill with concrete examples
   207→2. Plan reusable skill contents (scripts, references, assets)
   208→3. Initialize the skill (run init_skill.py)
   209→4. Edit the skill (implement resources and write SKILL.md)
   210→5. Package the skill (run package_skill.py)
   211→6. Iterate based on real usage
   212→
   213→Follow these steps in order, skipping only if there is a clear reason why they are not applicable.
   214→
   215→### Step 1: Understanding the Skill with Concrete Examples
   216→
   217→Skip this step only when the skill's usage patterns are already clearly understood. It remains valuable even when working with an existing skill.
   218→
   219→To create an effective skill, clearly understand concrete examples of how the skill will be used. This understanding can come from either direct user examples or generated examples that are validated with user feedback.
   220→
   221→For example, when building an image-editor skill, relevant questions include:
   222→
   223→- "What functionality should the image-editor skill support? Editing, rotating, anything else?"
   224→- "Can you give some examples of how this skill would be used?"
   225→- "I can imagine users asking for things like 'Remove the red-eye from this image' or 'Rotate this image'. Are there other ways you imagine this skill being used?"
   226→- "What would a user say that should trigger this skill?"
   227→
   228→To avoid overwhelming users, avoid asking too many questions in a single message. Start with the most important questions and follow up as needed for better effectiveness.
   229→
   230→Conclude this step when there is a clear sense of the functionality the skill should support.
   231→
   232→### Step 2: Planning the Reusable Skill Contents
   233→
   234→To turn concrete examples into an effective skill, analyze each example by:
   235→
   236→1. Considering how to execute on the example from scratch
   237→2. Identifying what scripts, references, and assets would be helpful when executing these workflows repeatedly
   238→
   239→Example: When building a `pdf-editor` skill to handle queries like "Help me rotate this PDF," the analysis shows:
   240→
   241→1. Rotating a PDF requires re-writing the same code each time
   242→2. A `scripts/rotate_pdf.py` script would be helpful to store in the skill
   243→
   244→Example: When designing a `frontend-webapp-builder` skill for queries like "Build me a todo app" or "Build me a dashboard to track my steps," the analysis shows:
   245→
   246→1. Writing a frontend webapp requires the same boilerplate HTML/React each time
   247→2. An `assets/hello-world/` template containing the boilerplate HTML/React project files would be helpful to store in the skill
   248→
   249→Example: When building a `big-query` skill to handle queries like "How many users have logged in today?" the analysis shows:
   250→
   251→1. Querying BigQuery requires re-discovering the table schemas and relationships each time
   252→2. A `references/schema.md` file documenting the table schemas would be helpful to store in the skill
   253→
   254→To establish the skill's contents, analyze each concrete example to create a list of the reusable resources to include: scripts, references, and assets.
   255→
   256→### Step 3: Initializing the Skill
   257→
   258→At this point, it is time to actually create the skill.
   259→
   260→Skip this step only if the skill being developed already exists, and iteration or packaging is needed. In this case, continue to the next step.
   261→
   262→When creating a new skill from scratch, always run the `init_skill.py` script. The script conveniently generates a new template skill directory that automatically includes everything a skill requires, making the skill creation process much more efficient and reliable.
   263→
   264→Usage:
   265→
   266→```bash
   267→scripts/init_skill.py <skill-name> --path <output-directory>
   268→```
   269→
   270→The script:
   271→
   272→- Creates the skill directory at the specified path
   273→- Generates a SKILL.md template with proper frontmatter and TODO placeholders
   274→- Creates example resource directories: `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/`
   275→- Adds example files in each directory that can be customized or deleted
   276→
   277→After initialization, customize or remove the generated SKILL.md and example files as needed.
   278→
   279→### Step 4: Edit the Skill
   280→
   281→When editing the (newly-generated or existing) skill, remember that the skill is being created for another instance of Claude to use. Include information that would be beneficial and non-obvious to Claude. Consider what procedural knowledge, domain-specific details, or reusable assets would help another Claude instance execute these tasks more effectively.
   282→
   283→#### Learn Proven Design Patterns
   284→
   285→Consult these helpful guides based on your skill's needs:
   286→
   287→- **Multi-step processes**: See references/workflows.md for sequential workflows and conditional logic
   288→- **Specific output formats or quality standards**: See references/output-patterns.md for template and example patterns
   289→
   290→These files contain established best practices for effective skill design.
   291→
   292→#### Start with Reusable Skill Contents
   293→
   294→To begin implementation, start with the reusable resources identified above: `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/` files. Note that this step may require user input. For example, when implementing a `brand-guidelines` skill, the user may need to provide brand assets or templates to store in `assets/`, or documentation to store in `references/`.
   295→
   296→Added scripts must be tested by actually running them to ensure there are no bugs and that the output matches what is expected. If there are many similar scripts, only a representative sample needs to be tested to ensure confidence that they all work while balancing time to completion.
   297→
   298→Any example files and directories not needed for the skill should be deleted. The initialization script creates example files in `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/` to demonstrate structure, but most skills won't need all of them.
   299→
   300→#### Update SKILL.md
   301→
   302→**Writing Guidelines:** Always use imperative/infinitive form.
   303→
   304→##### Frontmatter
   305→
   306→Write the YAML frontmatter with `name` and `description`:
   307→
   308→- `name`: The skill name
   309→- `description`: This is the primary triggering mechanism for your skill, and helps Claude understand when to use the skill.
   310→  - Include both what the Skill does and specific triggers/contexts for when to use it.
   311→  - Include all "when to use" information here - Not in the body. The body is only loaded after triggering, so "When to Use This Skill" sections in the body are not helpful to Claude.
   312→  - Example description for a `docx` skill: "Comprehensive document creation, editing, and analysis with support for tracked changes, comments, formatting preservation, and text extraction. Use when Claude needs to work with professional documents (.docx files) for: (1) Creating new documents, (2) Modifying or editing content, (3) Working with tracked changes, (4) Adding comments, or any other document tasks"
   313→
   314→Do not include any other fields in YAML frontmatter.
   315→
   316→##### Body
   317→
   318→Write instructions for using the skill and its bundled resources.
   319→
   320→### Step 5: Packaging a Skill
   321→
   322→Once development of the skill is complete, it must be packaged into a distributable .skill file that gets shared with the user. The packaging process automatically validates the skill first to ensure it meets all requirements:
   323→
   324→```bash
   325→scripts/package_skill.py <path/to/skill-folder>
   326→```
   327→
   328→Optional output directory specification:
   329→
   330→```bash
   331→scripts/package_skill.py <path/to/skill-folder> ./dist
   332→```
   333→
   334→The packaging script will:
   335→
   336→1. **Validate** the skill automatically, checking:
   337→
   338→   - YAML frontmatter format and required fields
   339→   - Skill naming conventions and directory structure
   340→   - Description completeness and quality
   341→   - File organization and resource references
   342→
   343→2. **Package** the skill if validation passes, creating a .skill file named after the skill (e.g., `my-skill.skill`) that includes all files and maintains the proper directory structure for distribution. The .skill file is a zip file with a .skill extension.
   344→
   345→If validation fails, the script will report the errors and exit without creating a package. Fix any validation errors and run the packaging command again.
   346→
   347→### Step 6: Iterate
   348→
   349→After testing the skill, users may request improvements. Often this happens right after using the skill, with fresh context of how the skill performed.
   350→
   351→**Iteration workflow:**
   352→
   353→1. Use the skill on real tasks
   354→2. Notice struggles or inefficiencies
   355→3. Identify how SKILL.md or bundled resources should be updated
   356→4. Implement changes and test again
   357→
```

     7→
     8→You are a Senior Sprint Analytics Specialist with deep expertise in JIRA sprint analysis, agile methodologies, and technical project management for recommendation systems. You specialize in transforming raw sprint data into actionable business intelligence that drives strategic decision-making.
     9→
    10→Your primary responsibilities:
    11→
    12→**Data Collection & Integration:**
    13→
    14→- Use MCP (Model Context Protocol) to connect to JIRA and fetch comprehensive sprint data including tickets, story points, assignees, dependencies, blockers, and status transitions
    15→- Extract metadata such as labels, components, fix versions, and custom fields relevant to the Recommendation Team
    16→
    17→**Business Contextualization:**
    18→
    19→1. **Sprint Data Analysis**: Connect to JIRA via MCP to fetch:
    20→   - All tickets in the specified sprint
    21→   - Ticket details (status, assignee, labels, components, description, story points, repositories mentioned)
    22→   - Epic relationships and business context
    23→   - Dependencies and blockers
    24→
    25→2. **Business Context Extraction**: For each ticket, identify:
    26→   - Business goals and impact
    27→   - Description of technical implementation
    28→   - JIRA ticket links
    29→   - Figma links (if available)
    30→   - A/B testing requirements
    31→   - Repository involvement
    32→
    33→**Identify these business scenarios:**
    34→
    35→- Hard deadlines (promos, launches)
    36→- Feature launches with high business impact
    37→- High-risk technical implementations
    38→- A/B testing requirements
    39→- Technical debt that blocks features
    40→- Cross-team dependencies
    41→
    42→**Report Example:**
    43→
    44→```

    49→1. Feature 1
    50→    - Business Goal: ***;
    51→    - Description: ***;
    52→    - A/B Tests Needed: ***;
    53→    - Repositories: ***;
    54→    - Figma: ***;
    55→    - JIRA: ***;
    56→2. Feature 2
    57→    - Business Goal: ***;
    58→    - Description: ***;
    59→    - A/B Tests Needed: ***;
    60→    - Repositories: ***;
    61→    - Figma: ***;
    62→    - JIRA: ***;
    63→
    64→Tasks to Note (Cross-Team Dependencies):
    65→    - [TICKET_ID]: [TITLE] - [Dependency Details]
    66→    - [TICKET_ID]: [TITLE] - [Dependency Details]
    67→Cross-Team Dependencies:
    68→    - Team A: [TICKET_ID] - [Dependency Details]
    69→    - Team B: [TICKET_ID] - [Dependency Details]
    70→
    71→```
    72→
    73→**Communication Standards:**
    74→
    75→- Use clear, jargon-free language for business stakeholders while maintaining technical accuracy
    76→- Provide visual data representations when beneficial (describe charts/graphs to be created)
    77→- Structure information hierarchically from high-level insights to detailed technical analysis
    78→- Include specific JIRA ticket references and links for traceability
    79→
    80→When generating reports
    81→
    82→- Follow the provided report structure strictly
    83→- Ensure all relevant business context is included for each ticket
    84→- Highlight critical dependencies and blockers clearly
    85→- Use bullet points and numbered lists for clarity
    86→- Avoid unnecessary technical jargon; focus on business impact and actionable insights
    87→- Do not add any suggestions or additional commentary outside the report structure
    88→

{

  "content": "---\nname: auto-developer\ndescription: Autonomous developer agent that continuously monitors the JIRA board, picks up tickets, implements them end-to-end (plan, code, test, fix, PR), and handles PR feedback. Use when user says \"auto-develop\", \"start auto mode\", \"autopilot\", \"work through the board\", \"pick up tickets automatically\", or wants Claude to autonomously process JIRA tickets in a loop. Combines JIRA monitoring, implementation, testing, and GitHub PR management into an infinite development loop.\n---\n\n# Auto-Developer\n\nAutonomous infinite-loop developer that monitors JIRA, picks up tickets, implements them, and pushes PRs — handling CI failures and review comments automatically.\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                   AUTO-DEVELOPER LOOP                │\n│                                                      │\n│  ┌──────────┐    ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────────┐  │\n│  │  JIRA    │───>│  PICK    │───>│  IMPLEMENT    │  │\n│  │  POLL    │    │  TICKET  │    │  (plan+code)  │  │\n│  └──────────┘    └──────────┘    └───────┬───────┘  │\n│       ▲                                  │          │\n│       │                                  ▼          │\n│  ┌──────────┐    ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────────┐  │\n│  │  NEXT    │<───│  PR      │<───│  VERIFY       │  │\n│  │  TICKET  │    │  CREATE  │    │  (lint+test)  │  │\n│  └──────────┘    └────┬─────┘    └───────────────┘  │\n│                       │                              │\n│                       ▼                              │\n│                  ┌──────────┐                        │\n│                  │  MONITOR │──> fix checks/comments │\n│                  │  PR      │──> push updates        │\n│                  └──────────┘                        │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Atlassian MCP plugin enabled (ATLASSIAN_CLOUD_ID in .env)\n- `gh` CLI authenticated\n- Clean git working tree (or stashable state)\n\n## Invocation\n\nUser provides:\n- **JQL filter** (optional): Custom JQL to select tickets. Default: `project = RECO AND status = \"To Do\" AND sprint in openSprints() ORDER BY priority DESC, created ASC`\n- **Poll interval** (optional): How often to check for new tickets. Default: `15m`\n- **Max tickets** (optional): Maximum tickets to process before stopping. Default: unlimited\n- **Ticket key** (optional): Specific ticket to start with (skips polling)\n\n## Main Loop\n\n### Phase 1: Setup & Poll\n\n1. Read Atlassian Cloud ID:\n   ```bash\n   grep ATLASSIAN_CLOUD_ID .env | cut -d'=' -f2\n   ```\n\n2. If specific ticket provided → skip to Phase 2 with that ticket.\n\n3. Search JIRA for available tickets:\n   ```typescript\n   searchJiraIssuesUsingJql({\n     cloudId: CLOUD_ID,\n     jql: JQL_FILTER,\n     maxResults: 5\n   })\n   ```\n\n4. If no tickets found → schedule next poll using CronCreate (interval from config), then wait.\n\n5. Select highest-priority ticket from results.\n\n### Phase 2: Start Ticket\n\nExecute the starting-jira-tasks workflow:\n\n1. Transition ticket to \"In Progress\" (only if status is \"To Do\")\n2. Git: stash → checkout main → pull → checkout/create RECO-XXX branch\n3. Fetch full ticket details:\n   ```typescript\n   getJiraIssue({ cloudId: CLOUD_ID, issueIdOrKey: TICKET_KEY })\n   ```\n4. Extract: title, description, acceptance criteria, subtasks, linked issues, attachments\n\n### Phase 3: Plan\n\nSee [references/implementation-cycle.md](references/implementation-cycle.md) for detailed planning process.\n\n1. Analyze ticket requirements thoroughly\n2. Explore codebase — find relevant files, patterns, similar implementations\n3. Create a plan with discrete tasks using TaskCreate\n4. Present plan summary (logged, not blocking — auto mode continues)\n\n### Phase 4: Implement\n\nSee [references/implementation-cycle.md](references/implementation-cycle.md) for implementation details.\n\n1. Execute plan tasks sequentially\n2. Follow ALL project rules from CLAUDE.md:\n   - Named parameters (object destructured)\n   - No comments in code\n   - Import aliases only\n   - @LogExecution on important methods\n   - Read/write DB pattern\n   - Inspect existing files before creating new ones\n3. Mark tasks complete as you go\n\n### Phase 5: Verify & Fix\n\nRun full verification suite (verify skill pattern):\n\n1. `npm run code-quality-check` — fix lint/format/type errors\n2. `npm run test:unit` — fix failing unit tests\n3. `npm run test:e2e` — fix failing e2e tests\n\n**Retry logic:** Up to 3 fix-verify cycles per step. If still failing after 3 attempts:\n- Add JIRA comment explaining the blocker\n- Transition ticket to needs attention (if transition available)\n- Move to next ticket\n\n### Phase 6: Commit & Push\n\n1. Stage relevant files (never `.env`, credentials, or large binaries)\n2. Commit with message: `RECO-XXX: Description in imperative mood`\n3. Push branch to remote: `git push -u origin BRANCH_NAME`\n\n### Phase 7: Create PR\n\nFollow the create-pr skill pattern:\n\n1. Detect base branch (stacked branch detection)\n2. Fetch JIRA task details for PR body\n3. Fill PR template with specific content (not placeholders)\n4. Create **draft** PR:\n   ```bash\n   gh pr create --draft --base BASE_BRANCH --title \"RECO-XXX: title\" --body \"...\"\n   ```\n\n### Phase 8: Monitor PR\n\nSee [references/pr-monitoring.md](references/pr-monitoring.md) for detailed monitoring process.\n\n1. Wait for CI checks to complete:\n   ```bash\n   gh pr checks BRANCH_NAME --watch\n   ```\n\n2. If checks fail:\n   - Fetch failure details: `gh pr checks BRANCH_NAME`\n   - Read CI logs: `gh run view RUN_ID --log-failed`\n   - Fix the issues\n   - Commit + push\n   - Re-monitor (up to 3 cycles)\n\n3. If checks pass:\n   - Mark PR as ready for review: `gh pr ready BRANCH_NAME`\n   - Add JIRA comment with PR link\n\n### Phase 9: Handle Review Comments\n\nAfter PR is ready, periodically check for review comments:\n\n```bash\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/reviews\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/comments\n```\n\nFor each unresolved comment:\n1. Read the comment and understand the request\n2. Make the requested change\n3. Commit + push\n4. Reply to the comment explaining what was done\n\n### Phase 10: Next Ticket\n\n1. Update JIRA ticket — add comment with final status\n2. Return to Phase 1 to poll for next ticket\n3. If max tickets reached → stop and report summary\n\n## Safety Rails\n\n| Rule | Why |\n|------|-----|\n| Never force-push | Could destroy upstream work |\n| Never commit .env or secrets | Security |\n| Max 3 retry cycles per verification step | Prevent infinite fix loops |\n| Always create draft PRs first | Human review before merge |\n| Never merge PRs automatically | Humans approve and merge |\n| Log every major action | Audit trail |\n| Stop on ambiguous requirements | Ask rather than guess wrong |\n\n## Stopping the Loop\n\nThe loop stops when:\n- User types \"stop\" or presses Ctrl+C\n- Max tickets reached\n- A ticket is too ambiguous to proceed (asks user)\n- 3 consecutive tickets fail verification (possible systemic issue)\n\n## Output Format\n\nEach ticket cycle outputs a status line:\n\n```\n[RECO-XXX] Started → Planned (5 tasks) → Implemented → Verified ✓ → PR #123 created → Checks ✓ → Ready for review\n```\n\nFailed tickets:\n\n```\n[RECO-XXX] Started → Planned (3 tasks) → Implemented → Verify FAILED (unit tests, 3 attempts) → Blocked, JIRA commented\n```\n\n## Using with /loop\n\nTo set up recurring JIRA board monitoring after completing current work:\n\n```\n/loop 15m /auto-developer\n```\n\nThis creates a cron job that polls the board every 15 minutes. When a ticket is found, the full cycle runs. When no tickets are found, it sleeps until next poll.\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n| Component | Tool/Skill Used |\n|-----------|----------------|\n| JIRA read | `mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__searchJiraIssuesUsingJql` |\n| JIRA ticket | `mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__getJiraIssue` |\n| JIRA transition | `mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__getTransitionsForJiraIssue` |\n| JIRA comment | `mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__editJiraIssue` |\n| Git operations | Bash (git CLI) |\n| PR creation | `gh pr create` |\n| PR monitoring | `gh pr checks`, `gh run view` |\n| PR comments | `gh api repos/.../pulls/.../comments` |\n| Code quality | `npm run code-quality-check` |\n| Unit tests | `npm run test:unit` |\n| E2E tests | `npm run test:e2e` |\n| Planning | TaskCreate / TaskUpdate |\n| Codebase exploration | Agent (Explore type) |\n| Implementation | Agent (nestjs-agent type) |\n| Debugging | Agent (debug-agent type) |\n| Test writing | Agent (test-agent type) |\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.claude/skills/auto-developer/references/implementation-cycle.md",
  "content": "# Implementation Cycle Reference\n\nDetailed guide for Phases 3-4 (Plan & Implement) of the auto-developer workflow.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [Planning Process](#planning-process)\n- [Implementation Process](#implementation-process)\n- [Handling Different Ticket Types](#handling-different-ticket-types)\n- [When to Stop and Ask](#when-to-stop-and-ask)\n\n## Planning Process\n\n### 1. Requirement Analysis\n\nRead the JIRA ticket thoroughly. Extract:\n\n- **What**: Feature/fix/refactor description\n- **Why**: Business context, user impact\n- **Acceptance criteria**: Explicit list of conditions\n- **Scope boundaries**: What is NOT in scope\n- **Dependencies**: Other tickets, external systems\n\n### 2. Codebase Exploration\n\nUse Agent (Explore type) to understand:\n\n```\n- Where does similar functionality live?\n- What patterns does the codebase use for this type of work?\n- What existing code can be reused or extended?\n- What tests exist for similar features?\n- What database tables/models are involved?\n```\n\nKey areas to explore based on ticket type:\n\n| Ticket Type | Explore |\n|-------------|---------|\n| New endpoint | `src/modules/rest/` — find similar controller+service |\n| DB change | `src/migrations/`, `src/modules/db/` — find similar model |\n| CLI command | `src/modules/cli/commands/` — find similar command |\n| Bug fix | Git blame + test files for the affected area |\n| Refactor | All files that import/use the target code |\n\n### 3. Plan Creation\n\nCreate tasks using TaskCreate. Each task should be:\n\n- **Atomic**: One logical change\n- **Ordered**: Dependencies respected\n- **Testable**: Clear verification criteria\n\nExample task breakdown for a new endpoint:\n\n```\n1. Create DTO (base, create, update) in src/dto/\n2. Create interface in src/interfaces/\n3. Create DB model in src/modules/db/\n4. Create service in src/modules/rest/\n5. Create controller in src/modules/rest/\n6. Register module\n7. Write unit tests\n8. Write e2e tests\n9. Update documentation (if applicable)\n```\n\n## Implementation Process\n\n### General Rules (from CLAUDE.md)\n\nEvery file you create or modify MUST follow these rules:\n\n1. **No comments** in code (except ESLint disable)\n2. **Named parameters** — ALL functions use object destructured params\n3. **Import aliases** — `@modules/`, `@utils/`, `@config/`, etc.\n4. **Inspect existing files first** — match exact patterns from similar files\n5. **@LogExecution** — on important methods and 3rd party requests\n6. **Read/write DB pattern** — `knexRead` for SELECT, `knexWrite` for mutations\n7. **Explicit return types** — on all functions\n8. **Logger** — `private readonly logger = new Logger(ClassName.name)`\n\n### File Creation Order\n\nWhen creating new functionality, follow this order to avoid import errors:\n\n1. Constants/enums (if needed)\n2. Interfaces\n3. DTOs\n4. Models (database layer)\n5. Services (business logic)\n6. Controllers (HTTP layer)\n7. Module registration\n8. Tests\n\n### Implementation Strategy\n\n**For each task:**\n\n1. Read the target file (or the most similar existing file if creating new)\n2. Implement following exact patterns from existing code\n3. Run `npm run lint:fix && npm run format` after each file\n4. Move to next task\n\n**Parallel agent usage:**\n- Use nestjs-agent for complex NestJS patterns\n- Use debug-agent when hitting unexpected errors\n- Use test-agent for comprehensive test creation\n- Use database-agent for migration/schema work\n\n### Environment Variables\n\nIf the ticket requires new env vars, follow the COMPLETE 5-step process:\n\n1. Add to `.env`, `.env.test`, `.env.example`, `.env.test.example`\n2. Create constant in `src/constants/env-variables.constant.ts`\n3. Add validation in `src/configs/env-validations.config.ts`\n4. Add to `src/utils/get-env-file.util.ts`\n5. Add test in `test/env-config.module.e2e-spec.ts`\n\n### Database Migrations\n\nIf the ticket requires DB changes:\n\n- **Safe single-release changes**: nullable column, column with default, new table, new index\n- **3-phase required**: drop column, rename column, type change, add NOT NULL without default\n\nAlways check: will new code work against OLD schema? (ktool deploys code before migration)\n\n## Handling Different Ticket Types\n\n### New REST Endpoint\n\n1. Find the most similar existing endpoint in `src/modules/rest/`\n2. Copy the pattern: controller → service → model → dto → interface\n3. Register in the appropriate module\n4. Add Swagger decorators: `@ApiOperation`, `@ApiResponse`\n5. Unit test the service, e2e test the endpoint\n\n### Bug Fix\n\n1. Reproduce: understand the failing scenario from ticket\n2. Write a failing test first (if possible)\n3. Fix the code\n4. Verify the test passes\n5. Check for similar bugs in related code\n\n### Refactor / ESLint Fix\n\n1. Identify all affected files (use Grep extensively)\n2. Make changes file by file\n3. Update ALL call sites (callers of changed functions)\n4. Update ALL tests (mock expectations, assertions)\n5. Run full verify after each batch\n\n### CLI Command\n\n1. Find similar command in `src/modules/cli/commands/`\n2. Follow the exact pattern: command class + worker (if needed)\n3. Register in CLI module\n4. Test manually: `npm run cli:dev -- command-name`\n\n### Data Pipeline / Cron Job\n\n1. Check `src/modules/cli/commands/` for similar jobs\n2. Follow worker pattern if CPU-intensive\n3. Add @LogExecution on main method\n4. Handle errors gracefully (retry logic, Rollbar reporting)\n\n## When to Stop and Ask\n\nStop the autonomous loop and ask the user when:\n\n| Situation | Why |\n|-----------|-----|\n| Ticket description is vague or contradictory | Wrong implementation is worse than no implementation |\n| Ticket requires infrastructure changes (AWS, Redis config) | Can't be done locally |\n| Ticket requires secrets/credentials you don't have | Security boundary |\n| Architecture decision not covered by existing patterns | Needs human judgment |\n| Breaking database change (drop/rename) | Needs 3-phase plan approval |\n| Ticket depends on another unfinished ticket | Dependency chain |\n| 3+ test failures you can't resolve | Systemic issue needs human eyes |\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.claude/skills/auto-developer/references/pr-monitoring.md",
  "content": "# PR Monitoring Reference\n\nDetailed guide for Phases 7-9 (PR Creation, CI Monitoring, Review Handling) of the auto-developer workflow.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [PR Creation](#pr-creation)\n- [CI Check Monitoring](#ci-check-monitoring)\n- [Review Comment Handling](#review-comment-handling)\n- [Failure Recovery](#failure-recovery)\n\n## PR Creation\n\n### Base Branch Detection\n\nCritical for stacked branches. Detect correct base:\n\n```bash\n# Current branch\nCURRENT=$(git branch --show-current)\n\n# Find fork point with main\nFORK_POINT=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)\n\n# Find remote branches at fork point\ngit branch -r --contains $FORK_POINT | grep -v \"HEAD\" | grep -v \"$CURRENT\"\n\n# Check recent decorations for branch origin\ngit log --format='%D' HEAD | grep 'origin/' | grep -v 'HEAD' | head -5\n```\n\nRules:\n- Branch cut from `main` → base = `main`\n- Branch cut from `RECO-XXX-*` → base = that branch\n- Uncertain → default to `main` but log a warning\n\n### PR Body Generation\n\nRead the template and fill ALL sections:\n\n```bash\ncat .github/pull_request_template.md\n```\n\nFill with specific content from the implementation:\n- **Ticket**: JIRA link with ticket key\n- **Changes**: Every changed file with precise explanation\n- **PR Checks**: Check boxes that actually apply\n- **Description**: Business-level explanation from JIRA ticket\n- **Screenshots**: Omit for backend-only changes\n\n### PR Creation Command\n\n```bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --draft \\\n  --base BASE_BRANCH \\\n  --title \"RECO-XXX: Short imperative description\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n### Ticket\nJIRA task: [RECO-XXX](https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX)\n\n### Changes\n- **`path/to/file.ts`** — What changed and why\n- **`path/to/test.unit-spec.ts`** — Test cases added\n\n### PR Checks\n- [x] I've self-reviewed my code.\n- [x] I've added the necessary labels.\n- [x] I've added tests for my code.\n- [ ] I've updated the documentation.\n\n### Description / Ticket Summary\nConcise business-level explanation.\n\n### Screenshots / Short Videos\nN/A (backend-only)\nEOF\n)\"\n```\n\n## CI Check Monitoring\n\n### Watch Checks\n\nAfter PR creation, monitor CI:\n\n```bash\n# Watch checks until they complete (blocking)\ngh pr checks $(git branch --show-current) --watch --fail-fast\n```\n\nIf `--watch` is unavailable or times out, poll manually:\n\n```bash\n# Check status\ngh pr checks $(git branch --show-current)\n\n# Get PR number\nPR_NUM=$(gh pr view --json number -q '.number')\n\n# List check runs\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/$(git rev-parse HEAD)/check-runs\n```\n\n### Handling Check Failures\n\nWhen a check fails:\n\n1. **Identify the failing check:**\n   ```bash\n   gh pr checks $(git branch --show-current)\n   ```\n\n2. **Get the run ID and logs:**\n   ```bash\n   # List workflow runs for the branch\n   gh run list --branch $(git branch --show-current) --limit 5\n\n   # View failed run logs\n   gh run view RUN_ID --log-failed\n   ```\n\n3. **Common failure types and fixes:**\n\n   | Failure | Fix Strategy |\n   |---------|-------------|\n   | Lint errors | `npm run lint:fix` → commit → push |\n   | Type errors | Fix TypeScript issues → commit → push |\n   | Unit test failures | Read test output, fix code or test → commit → push |\n   | E2E test failures | Read test output, may need DB/env fix → commit → push |\n   | Build failure | Check compilation errors → fix → commit → push |\n   | Coverage below threshold | Add missing tests → commit → push |\n\n4. **Fix cycle:**\n   ```bash\n   # Make fixes locally\n   # ...\n\n   # Verify locally first\n   npm run code-quality-check\n   npm run test:unit\n   npm run test:e2e\n\n   # Commit and push\n   git add <specific-files>\n   git commit -m \"RECO-XXX: Fix CI — description of fix\"\n   git push\n\n   # Re-monitor\n   gh pr checks $(git branch --show-current) --watch\n   ```\n\n5. **Max 3 fix cycles.** If still failing:\n   - Comment on PR explaining the persistent failure\n   - Add JIRA comment\n   - Move to next ticket\n\n### Transition to Ready\n\nWhen all checks pass:\n\n```bash\n# Mark PR ready for review\ngh pr ready $(git branch --show-current)\n```\n\n## Review Comment Handling\n\n### Polling for Reviews\n\nAfter PR is ready for review, check periodically for feedback:\n\n```bash\n# Get PR number\nPR_NUM=$(gh pr view --json number -q '.number')\n\n# Fetch reviews\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/reviews\n\n# Fetch inline comments\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments\n```\n\n### Processing Review Comments\n\nFor each comment:\n\n1. **Read and understand** the reviewer's request\n2. **Categorize:**\n   - Code change requested → make the change\n   - Question → reply with explanation\n   - Nitpick/style → make the change (don't argue with reviewers)\n   - Architecture concern → flag to user (don't make large architectural changes autonomously)\n\n3. **Make changes:**\n   ```bash\n   # Edit files as requested\n   # ...\n\n   # Verify changes don't break anything\n   npm run code-quality-check\n   npm run test:unit\n\n   # Commit with descriptive message\n   git add <specific-files>\n   git commit -m \"RECO-XXX: Address review — description\"\n   git push\n   ```\n\n4. **Reply to comment:**\n   ```bash\n   gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments/$COMMENT_ID/replies \\\n     -f body=\"Done — [brief explanation of what was changed]\"\n   ```\n\n### Review Comment Limits\n\n- Max 3 rounds of review fixes per PR\n- If reviewer requests fundamental redesign → stop, ask user\n- If reviewer's request contradicts CLAUDE.md rules → flag to user\n- Never dismiss or resolve someone else's review comments programmatically\n\n## Failure Recovery\n\n### Git State Recovery\n\nIf something goes wrong mid-implementation:\n\n```bash\n# Check current state\ngit status\ngit stash list\n\n# If working tree is dirty and unrecoverable\ngit stash push -m \"auto-developer: saving state before recovery\"\n\n# If branch is in bad state, can start fresh\ngit checkout main\ngit pull\ngit checkout -b RECO-XXX-retry\n```\n\n### Stuck Detection\n\nThe auto-developer should recognize when it's stuck:\n\n- Same test failing after 3 fix attempts\n- Circular dependency in changes\n- Changes keep reverting each other\n- Unable to understand requirement\n\nWhen stuck → add JIRA comment, move to next ticket.\n\n### JIRA Status Management\n\nTrack progress via JIRA comments (do NOT change ticket status beyond \"In Progress\"):\n\n```\n[Auto-Developer] Implementation started\n[Auto-Developer] PR #123 created: https://github.com/...\n[Auto-Developer] CI checks passed, PR ready for review\n[Auto-Developer] BLOCKED: Unit tests failing after 3 attempts — [details]\n```\n\nHuman decides when to move ticket to \"Done\" or \"In Review\".\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/4thwithme/Documents/repo/recommendations-service/.claude/skills/auto-developer/references/ticket-selection.md",
  "content": "# Ticket Selection Reference\n\nHow the auto-developer selects and prioritizes JIRA tickets.\n\n## Table of Contents\n\n- [Default JQL Query](#default-jql-query)\n- [Priority Logic](#priority-logic)\n- [Ticket Eligibility](#ticket-eligibility)\n- [Skipping Tickets](#skipping-tickets)\n\n## Default JQL Query\n\n```jql\nproject = RECO\n  AND status = \"To Do\"\n  AND sprint in openSprints()\nORDER BY priority DESC, created ASC\n```\n\nThis finds: tickets in the current sprint, not yet started, ordered by priority then age.\n\n### Custom Filters\n\nUser can override with any JQL:\n\n```\n/auto-developer jql=\"project = RECO AND status = 'To Do' AND assignee = currentUser()\"\n/auto-developer jql=\"project = RECO AND labels = 'auto-ok' AND status = 'To Do'\"\n```\n\n### Useful JQL Variations\n\n| Scenario | JQL |\n|----------|-----|\n| Only my tickets | `AND assignee = currentUser()` |\n| Only labeled for auto | `AND labels = \"auto-ok\"` |\n| Specific epic | `AND \"Epic Link\" = RECO-XXX` |\n| Exclude blocked | `AND status != \"Blocked\"` |\n| Bug fixes only | `AND type = Bug` |\n| Small tasks only | `AND story_points <= 3` |\n\n## Priority Logic\n\nWhen multiple tickets match the query:\n\n1. **Highest JIRA priority** wins (Blocker > Critical > Major > Minor > Trivial)\n2. **Within same priority**, oldest ticket first (FIFO)\n3. **User override**: specific ticket key always takes precedence\n\n## Ticket Eligibility\n\nA ticket is eligible for auto-development when:\n\n| Criterion | Required | Why |\n|-----------|----------|-----|\n| Status is \"To Do\" | Yes | Only pick up unstarted work |\n| Has description | Yes | Need requirements to implement |\n| Has acceptance criteria | Preferred | Clearer done-definition |\n| No blockers | Yes | Can't implement blocked tickets |\n| No \"needs-design\" label | Yes | Requires human design first |\n| No \"needs-discussion\" label | Yes | Requires human input |\n| In current sprint | Default | Focus on current sprint work |\n\n## Skipping Tickets\n\nSkip a ticket and move to the next when:\n\n| Condition | Action |\n|-----------|--------|\n| Empty description | Skip, add JIRA comment: \"Skipped by auto-developer: no description\" |\n| Has \"needs-design\" label | Skip silently |\n| Has \"needs-discussion\" label | Skip silently |\n| Depends on unmerged PR | Skip, log dependency |\n| Requires infrastructure changes | Skip, add JIRA comment |\n| Description says \"TBD\" or \"TODO\" | Skip, add JIRA comment |\n| Ticket type is \"Epic\" | Skip (epics are containers, not work items) |\n\n### Ticket Readiness Check\n\nBefore starting implementation, verify:\n\n```\n1. Description exists and is non-empty\n2. Description is not just \"TBD\", \"TODO\", \"WIP\"\n3. No blocking labels (needs-design, needs-discussion, blocked)\n4. No unresolved blockers in linked issues\n5. Acceptance criteria are parseable (optional but preferred)\n```\n\nIf readiness check fails → skip ticket, add comment, try next.\n"
}
```

Skill created. Here's what was built:

## `/auto-developer` Skill

**Structure:**
```
.claude/skills/auto-developer/
├── SKILL.md                              # Main orchestrator (10 phases)
└── references/
    ├── implementation-cycle.md           # Planning + coding details
    ├── pr-monitoring.md                  # CI checks + review handling
    └── ticket-selection.md              # JIRA polling + priority logic
```

**10-Phase Loop:**

| Phase | What |
|-------|------|
| 1 | Poll JIRA board via JQL |
| 2 | Start ticket (transition + branch) |
| 3 | Plan (explore codebase, create tasks) |
| 4 | Implement (following all CLAUDE.md rules) |
| 5 | Verify & fix (lint, unit, e2e — max 3 retries) |
| 6 | Commit & push |
| 7 | Create draft PR (stacked branch detection) |
| 8 | Monitor CI checks, fix failures |
| 9 | Handle review comments |
| 10 | Next ticket |

**Safety rails:** Never force-push, never commit secrets, never merge PRs, max 3 retry cycles, always draft PRs, stops on ambiguous requirements.

**Usage:**
- `/auto-developer` — starts processing "To Do" tickets from current sprint
- `/auto-developer jql="..."` — custom ticket filter
- `/auto-developer RECO-123` — start with specific ticket
- `/loop 15m /auto-developer` — recurring board monitoring

**Integrates:** Atlassian MCP (JIRA), `gh` CLI (GitHub), existing skills (verify, create-pr, starting-jira-tasks patterns), subagents (explore, nestjs, debug, test).

- When user gives a list of tasks — do they give priority order, or do we follow JIRA priority?

**Step 3 — Progress tracker:**

**Step 9 — Tests:**
- 95% coverage threshold — is that for the **entire project** or just **files changed in this ticket**? If existing coverage is below 95% somewhere we didn't touch, do we fix that too?
- Unit and e2e separately must both be above 95%?

**Step 11 — PR creation:**
- For multi-phase releases (e.g., DB column drop = 3 PRs) — should the skill create all 3 PRs upfront with `[Phase 1]`, `[Phase 2]`, `[Phase 3]` titles? Or one at a time?

**Step 12 — PR monitoring:**

**General:**
- Should the orchestrator persist its state somewhere so if the session dies, a new session can resume from the tracker file?
- Max concurrent tasks (waiting for review)? Or strictly 1 active + 1 in review?
