     1→# Contributing
     2→
     3→Guidelines for contributing to the Recommendations Service.
     4→
     5→## Branches
     6→
     7→### Main Branch
     8→
     9→`main` - Protected. No direct pushes. Create a PR and get it approved.
    10→
    11→### Branch Naming
    12→
    13→`RECO-XXXX-suffix` - Where `RECO-XXXX` is the Jira ticket number and `suffix` is a short description.
    14→
    15→Example: `RECO-1234-fixing-bug`
    16→
    17→Other naming patterns are restricted.
    18→
    19→## Commits
    20→
    21→Commit messages must:
    22→
    23→- Start with the ticket prefix (branch name without suffix)
    24→- Use imperative mood
    25→- Be short and descriptive
    26→
    27→```
    28→RECO-1234: Add new feature        # correct
    29→RECO-1234: Added new feature      # wrong - not imperative
    30→```
    31→
    32→## Pull Requests
    33→
    34→> **WARN**: Before creating a PR, make sure your branch is up to date with `main`.
    35→
    36→> **WARN**: Before merging, your PR **must be approved** by at least one Code Owner.
    37→
    38→### PR Title
    39→
    40→`RECO-XXXX: Short description` - Same prefix as branch, without suffix.
    41→
    42→### Code Review
    43→
    44→- Assign at least two Code Owners
    45→- Get at least one Code Owner approval
    46→
    47→### Merge Policy
    48→
    49→- Only **squash merge** to `main`
    50→- For dependent branches, merge or squash merge is allowed
    51→- Code Owner approval required before merge
    52→
    53→## Database Migrations
    54→
    55→> **CRITICAL**: Before writing any migration that modifies existing columns or tables,
    56→> read [docs/database/zero-downtime-migrations.md](./docs/database/zero-downtime-migrations.md).
    57→
    58→### Deployment Order
    59→
    60→ktool deploys **new code before running migrations**:
    61→
    62→```
    63→1. New pods start        ← new code, old schema (migration hasn't run)
    64→2. Health checks pass
    65→3. Migration runs        ← schema changes while both pod versions are live
    66→4. Old pods drain        ← old code briefly runs against new schema
    67→```
    68→
    69→New code must work with the old schema. Old code must work with the new schema.
    70→
    71→### Breaking Changes Require 3 Releases
    72→
    73→The following changes **cannot** be made in a single release:
    74→
    75→| Change                   | Required strategy                                          |
    76→| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
    77→| Drop column              | Phase 1: stop using → Phase 3: drop                        |
    78→| Rename column            | Phase 1: add new → Phase 2: dual-write → Phase 3: drop old |
    79→| Incompatible type change | Phase 1: add new → Phase 2: dual-write → Phase 3: drop old |
    80→| NOT NULL without default | Phase 1: nullable → Phase 2: backfill → Phase 3: constrain |
    81→| Drop table               | Phase 1: remove code refs → Phase 3: drop                  |
    82→
    83→**Safe in a single release**: add nullable column, add table, add index, widen column.
    84→
    85→### PR Title for 3-Phase Changes
    86→
    87→```
    88→RECO-1234 [Phase 1]: Add supplier_name to styles_mv
    89→RECO-1234 [Phase 2]: Dual-write supplier_name, backfill rows
    90→RECO-1234 [Phase 3]: Drop old_supplier_col from styles_mv
    91→```
    92→
    93→Link all 3 Jira tickets to each other. Never merge Phase 3 until Phase 2 has run
    94→through at least one full cron job cycle in production.
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→## Environment Variables
    99→
   100→Environment variables are stored in `.env` (gitignored). See `.env.example` for reference.
   101→
   102→### Adding a New Environment Variable
   103→
   104→1. **Add to env files**: `.env`, `.env.test`, `.env.example`, `.env.test.example`
   105→
   106→2. **Create constant** in `src/constants/env-variables.constant.ts`:
   107→
   108→   ```typescript
   109→   export const NEW_VARIABLE_NAME = 'NEW_VARIABLE_NAME';
   110→   ```
   111→
   112→3. **Add validation** in `src/configs/env-validations.config.ts`:
   113→
   114→   ```typescript
   115→   @IsString()
   116→   @IsNotEmpty()
   117→   NEW_VARIABLE_NAME: string;
   118→   ```
   119→
   120→4. **Add to loader** in `src/utils/get-env-file.util.ts`:
   121→
   122→   ```typescript
   123→   NEW_VARIABLE_NAME: process.env['NEW_VARIABLE_NAME'],
   124→   ```
   125→
   126→5. **Add test** in `test/env-config.module.e2e-spec.ts`
   127→
   128→### Usage
   129→
   130→**Option A**: `EnvConfigService` (dependency injection):
   131→
   132→```typescript
   133→const value = this.configService.get<string>({ key: NEW_VARIABLE_NAME });
   134→```
   135→
   136→**Option B**: `getEnv` utility (standalone):
   137→
   138→```typescript
   139→const value = getEnv<string>({ key: NEW_VARIABLE_NAME });
   140→```
   141→
   142→**Never** access `process.env` directly in application code. Always use constants from `@constants/env-variables.constant`.
   143→
   144→## Logging
   145→
   146→Use the built-in NestJS `Logger`:
   147→
   148→```typescript
   149→import { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';
   150→
   151→export class ClassName {
   152→	private readonly logger = new Logger(ClassName.name);
   153→}
   154→```
   155→
   156→Dev configuration:
   157→
   158→```bash
   159→IS_COMPACT_LOGS=false
   160→IS_JSON_LIKE_LOGS=true
   161→```
   162→
   163→On staging/production, `IS_COMPACT_LOGS` should be `true`.
   164→
   165→## Import Aliases
   166→
   167→Use path aliases instead of relative imports:
   168→
   169→| Alias               | Path                   |
   170→| ------------------- | ---------------------- |
   171→| `@config/*`         | `src/configs/*`        |
   172→| `@constants/*`      | `src/constants/*`      |
   173→| `@modules/*`        | `src/modules/*`        |
   174→| `@utils/*`          | `src/utils/*`          |
   175→| `@middlewares/*`    | `src/middlewares/*`    |
   176→| `@services/*`       | `src/services/*`       |
   177→| `@interfaces/*`     | `src/interfaces/*`     |
   178→| `@decorators/*`     | `src/decorators/*`     |
   179→| `@dto/*`            | `src/dto/*`            |
   180→| `@guards/*`         | `src/guards/*`         |
   181→| `@interceptors/*`   | `src/interceptors/*`   |
   182→| `@frontend-tests/*` | `src/frontend-tests/*` |
   183→| `@seeds/*`          | `src/seeds/*`          |
   184→
   185→Full list in `tsconfig.json`.
   186→
   187→## API Documentation (Swagger)
   188→
   189→Available at `localhost:9009/swagger` during development.
   190→
   191→When adding new endpoints, include Swagger decorators:
   192→
   193→```typescript
   194→@ApiOperation({ summary: 'Service liveness probe' })
   195→@ApiResponse({
   196→  status: HttpStatus.OK,
   197→  description: 'Return status 200 if the application is live',
   198→})
   199→@Get('/liveness')
   200→appHealthCheck(@Res() response: Response) {
   201→  // code
   202→}
   203→```
   204→
   205→## Documentation Policy
   206→
   207→Every PR that introduces new functionality or modifies existing logic **must** include a corresponding update or creation of documentation in the `/docs` folder.
   208→
   209→- New features → create a new doc or add a section to an existing one
   210→- Changed logic → update the relevant existing doc
   211→- No feature is considered complete without documentation
   212→
   213→See the [`docs/`](./docs) directory for existing documentation structure.
   214→
   215→## RFC (Request for Comments)
   216→
   217→RFCs describe new features or changes. Create them in the `rfc/` folder following existing patterns. Include sequence diagrams, flowcharts, and visual information where possible.
   218→

set -euo pipefail

# ─────────────────────────────────────────────
# new-worktree.sh
# Usage: ./scripts/new-worktree.sh RECO-888
#        ./scripts/new-worktree.sh RECO-888-my-feature
#        ./scripts/new-worktree.sh RECO-ESLINT   (no number → random free port)
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────

BRANCH_NAME="${1:-}"

if [[ -z "$BRANCH_NAME" ]]; then
  echo "Error: branch name required. Usage: $0 RECO-888" >&2
  exit 1
fi

# Find free port helper — scans sequentially from a random start
find_free_port() {
  local start=$1
  local end=$2
  local port=$(( start + RANDOM % (end - start) ))
  while (( port <= end )); do
    if ! nc -z localhost "$port" 2>/dev/null; then
      echo "$port"
      return
    fi
    (( port++ ))
  done
  echo "Error: no free port found in range $start-$end" >&2
  exit 1
}

# Extract RECO number for port calculation (first sequence of digits)
RECO_NUM=$(echo "$BRANCH_NAME" | grep -oE '[0-9]+' | head -1)

REPO_ROOT="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
PARENT_DIR="$(dirname "$REPO_ROOT")"
REPO_BASENAME="$(basename "$REPO_ROOT")"
WORKTREE_DIR="${PARENT_DIR}/${REPO_BASENAME}-${BRANCH_NAME}"

if [[ -n "$RECO_NUM" ]]; then
  # Numeric ticket: deterministic port + named task ID
  PORT=$((10000 + RECO_NUM))
  if lsof -iTCP:"$PORT" -sTCP:LISTEN -t > /dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "Warning: port $PORT busy, finding free port..." >&2
    PORT=$(find_free_port 11000 19999)
  fi
  TASK_ID="reco-${RECO_NUM}"
else
  # No number (e.g. RECO-ESLINT): random free port + slug-based task ID
  PORT=$(find_free_port 11000 19999)
  TASK_ID=$(echo "$BRANCH_NAME" | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]' | tr '_' '-')
fi

echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"
echo "  new-worktree: $BRANCH_NAME"
echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"
echo "  Dir        : $WORKTREE_DIR"
echo "  Port       : $PORT"
echo "  Redis ns   : $TASK_ID:development:*"
echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"

# ── 1. Check not already exists ──────────────
if git worktree list | grep -q "$WORKTREE_DIR"; then
  echo "Error: worktree '$WORKTREE_DIR' already exists." >&2
  exit 1
fi

if [[ -d "$WORKTREE_DIR" ]]; then
  echo "Error: directory '$WORKTREE_DIR' already exists." >&2
  exit 1
fi

# ── 2. Create worktree ────────────────────────
echo ""
echo "[1/4] Creating git worktree..."
if git show-ref --verify --quiet "refs/heads/$BRANCH_NAME"; then
  git worktree add "$WORKTREE_DIR" "$BRANCH_NAME"
else
  git worktree add "$WORKTREE_DIR" -b "$BRANCH_NAME"
fi

# ── 3. Copy and patch .env ────────────────────
echo "[2/4] Configuring .env..."
cp "${REPO_ROOT}/.env" "${WORKTREE_DIR}/.env"

# PORT
sed -i '' "s|^PORT=.*|PORT=${PORT}|" "${WORKTREE_DIR}/.env"

# SERVER_NAME → used by CacheModule as Redis key namespace: "reco-888:development:*"
sed -i '' "s|^SERVER_NAME=.*|SERVER_NAME=${TASK_ID}|" "${WORKTREE_DIR}/.env"

# ── 4. Install node modules ───────────────────
echo "[3/4] Installing node modules (this takes a moment)..."
cd "$WORKTREE_DIR"
npm install --silent

# ── 5. Open Warp tab (optional) ──────────────
if [[ -d "/Applications/Warp.app" ]] && pgrep -x "stable" > /dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "[4/4] Opening Warp tab..."
  printf "printf $'\\\\033]0;WT %s\\\\007' && cd %s && claude" "$BRANCH_NAME" "$WORKTREE_DIR" | pbcopy

  osascript << APPLESCRIPT
tell application "Warp" to activate
delay 0.8
tell application "System Events"
  tell process "Warp"
    keystroke "t" using command down
    delay 1.5
    keystroke "v" using command down
    delay 0.5
    key code 36
  end tell
end tell
APPLESCRIPT
else
  echo "[4/4] Skipping Warp tab (not installed or not running)."
  echo "  cd $WORKTREE_DIR && claude"
fi

echo ""
echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"
echo "  Worktree ready! Claude launched in Warp."
echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"
echo "  PORT        = $PORT"
echo "  SERVER_NAME = $TASK_ID  (Redis ns: $TASK_ID:development:*)"
echo "  DATABASE_URL = shared"
echo ""
echo "  Remove when done:"
echo "    git worktree remove $WORKTREE_DIR"
echo "    git branch -d $BRANCH_NAME"
echo "━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━"
```

    10→## Workflow

    54→## Common Issues

     1→---
     2→name: security-reviewer
     3→description: Reviews code for security vulnerabilities — API key exposure, auth bypass, injection risks, insecure env var handling, and Redis/SQS security patterns. Use when adding new endpoints, auth logic, external API integrations, or handling sensitive data.
     4→tools: Read, Grep, Glob
     5→---
     6→
     7→You are a security reviewer for a NestJS/TypeScript microservice that handles product recommendations. Review code strictly and report findings with severity.
     8→
     9→## What to check
    10→
    11→**Secrets & env vars**
    12→- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, or secrets in source files
    13→- Direct `process.env` access (project mandates `EnvConfigService` or `getEnv()` utility)
    14→- Secrets logged via `@LogExecution` without being in `sensitiveKeys` list
    15→
    16→**Auth & access control**
    17→- Missing `@UseGuards()` on REST controllers or routes that require auth
    18→- Auth guard logic that can be bypassed (e.g., incorrect token comparison, missing null checks)
    19→- Routes exposed under `/api/v1/` without rate limiting (`@Throttle`)
    20→
    21→**Injection risks**
    22→- Raw SQL strings passed to `knex.raw()` without parameterization
    23→- User-controlled input used in Algolia query filters without sanitization
    24→- SQS message bodies deserialized without schema validation
    25→
    26→**Data exposure**
    27→- Sensitive fields (prices, PII, internal IDs) returned in API responses without explicit DTO exclusion
    28→- `@LogExecution` logging full responses that contain sensitive data
    29→
    30→**Redis & SQS**
    31→- Redis keys constructed from user input without sanitization
    32→- Missing TTL on cache entries storing sensitive data
    33→- SQS messages processed without idempotency checks
    34→
    35→## Output format
    36→
    37→Report each finding as:
    38→
    39→```
    40→SEVERITY: HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW
    41→FILE: path/to/file.ts:line
    42→ISSUE: What the vulnerability is
    43→FIX: Specific remediation
    44→```
    45→
    46→Severities:
    47→- **HIGH**: Exploitable in production (hardcoded secret, SQL injection, auth bypass)
    48→- **MEDIUM**: Risk under specific conditions (missing rate limit, logged sensitive data)
    49→- **LOW**: Defense-in-depth improvement (missing TTL, overly broad response DTO)
    50→
    51→If no issues found, output: `No security issues found.`
    52→
```

Read the implementation plan\n2. Follow the task breakdown in order\n3. For each file: read the most similar existing file FIRST, then match its patterns exactly\n4. Rules (non-negotiable):\n   - No comments in code (except ESLint disable)\n   - Named parameters (object destructured) for ALL functions\n   - Import aliases only (`@modules/`, `@utils/`, etc.)\n   - `@LogExecution` on important methods and 3rd party requests\n   - `knexRead` for SELECT, `knexWrite` for INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE\n   - Explicit return types on all functions\n   - `Logger` from `@nestjs/common`, never `console.log`\n   - Swagger decorators on all endpoints\n5. If new env vars needed → complete ALL 5 steps from CLAUDE.md\n6. Commit incrementally with `RECO-XXX: description` format\n\n---\n\n### Step 9: Code Quality Check #1\n\n**Goal:** Ensure code passes all quality gates.\n\nSpawn Sonnet subagent in the worktree:\n\n```bash\nnpm run lint:fix\nnpm run format\nnpm run type-check\nnpm run code-quality-check   # final verification\n```\n\n**Fix loop:** If errors remain → fix them → re-run. Max 5 cycles.\nIf critical implementation flaws found → spawn new subagent to fix the implementation, then re-check.\n\n---\n\n### Step 10: Tests (Write + Fix + Coverage)\n\n**Goal:** 95% coverage for BOTH unit AND e2e tests independently. All tests pass.\n\nSpawn Sonnet subagent (use test-agent patterns from `.claude/agents/test-agent.md`).\n\n**Working directory:** Worktree.\n\n**Process:**\n\n1. Run existing tests first to check baseline:\n   ```bash\n   npm run test:unit:coverage\n   npm run test:e2e:coverage\n   ```\n\n2. Analyze coverage reports — identify gaps in files touched by this ticket AND project-wide\n\n3. Write new tests:\n   - Unit tests in `/src/unit-tests/*.unit-spec.ts`\n   - E2E tests in `/test/*.e2e-spec.ts`\n\n4. Fix broken tests\n\n5. Re-run coverage until:\n   - `npm run test:unit:coverage` → ≥95% branches/functions/lines/statements\n   - `npm run test:e2e:coverage` → ≥95% branches/functions/lines/statements\n\n6. All tests must pass (not just coverage)\n\n**Max 5 fix-test cycles.** If still failing → log to tracker, flag to user.\n\n---\n\n### Step 11: Code Quality Check #2\n\n**Goal:** Post-test quality verification.\n\nSpawn Sonnet subagent in worktree:\n\n```bash\nnpm run lint\nnpm run format\nnpm run type-check\n```\n\nFix until all three pass with zero errors.\n\n---\n\n### Step 12: Security Review\n\n**Goal:** No security vulnerabilities in the code.\n\nSpawn subagent using security-reviewer patterns (`.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md`).\n\nCheck for:\n- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, secrets in source files\n- Direct `process.env` access (must use `EnvConfigService` or `getEnv()`)\n- Secrets logged via `@LogExecution` without `sensitiveKeys` masking\n- Missing `@UseGuards()` on auth-required routes\n- Raw SQL without parameterization in `knex.raw()`\n- User input in Redis keys without sanitization\n- Sensitive data in API responses without DTO exclusion\n- `.env` files or credentials staged for commit\n\n**If HIGH severity found:** Block PR creation. Detect base branch:**\n- If branch cut from `main` → base = `main`\n- If stacked on another feature branch → base = that branch\n- Use the pattern from create-pr skill\n\n**13b. Multi-phase check:**\nIf implementation plan says multi-phase → create PRs one at a time:\n```\nRECO-XXX [Phase 1]: Add new_col to table_name\nRECO-XXX [Phase 2]: Dual-write new_col, backfill rows\nRECO-XXX [Phase 3]: Drop old_col from table_name\n```\nCreate Phase 1 PR first. Phases 2 and 3 are created after Phase 1 merges.\n\n**13c. Push and create PR:**\n```bash\ngit push -u origin BRANCH_NAME\n\ngh pr create \\\n  --draft \\\n  --base BASE_BRANCH \\\n  --title \"RECO-XXX: Short description\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n### Ticket 🐗\nJIRA task: [RECO-XXX](https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX)\nDesign: [Figma Link](url) — omit if none\n\n### Changes 🍍\n- **`path/to/file.ts`** — What changed and why\n- **`path/to/test.unit-spec.ts`** — Test cases added\n\n### PR Checks 🐊\n- [x] I've self-reviewed my code.\n- [x] I've added the necessary labels.\n- [x] I've added tests for my code.\n- [x/blank] I've updated the documentation.\n\n### Description / Ticket Summary 🐙\nBusiness-level explanation of what and why.\n\n### Screenshots / Short Videos 🐮\nN/A (backend-only) or screenshots if UI changes.\nEOF\n)\"\n```\n\nSave PR URL to progress tracker.\n\n---\n\n### Step 14: JIRA → Code Review + Monitor Loop\n\n**Goal:** Transition JIRA, monitor for human feedback, handle it, clean up.\n\n**14a. Transition JIRA to \"Code Review\":**\n```typescript\n// Find \"Code Review\" transition and execute\n```\n\n**14b. Add JIRA comment:**\n```\n🐒 PR created: [PR_URL]\nWaiting for human review.\n```\n\n**14c. Set up monitoring loop:**\n\nUse `/loop 10m` or CronCreate to poll every 10 minutes:\n\n```bash\n# Check for new review comments (from humans, not bots)\nPR_NUM=$(gh pr view --json number -q '.number')\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/reviews\ngh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments\n\n# Check PR status\ngh pr checks $(git branch --show-current)\n```\n\n**14d. Handle review comments:**\n\nFor each new human comment:\n1. Read and understand the request\n2. Categorize:\n   - Code change → spawn Sonnet agent to fix → re-run steps 9, 10, 11\n   - Question → reply with explanation\n   - Architecture concern → flag to user, don't change autonomously\n3. Commit + push changes\n4. Reply to comment:\n   ```bash\n   gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments/$COMMENT_ID/replies \\\n     -f body=\"🐒 Done — [what was changed]\"\n   ```\n\n**14e. Handle CI failures:**\n- Read failed check logs: `gh run view RUN_ID --log-failed`\n- Spawn fix agent → re-run quality + tests → push\n- Max 3 fix cycles\n\n**14f. On PR approval + merge-ready:**\n1. Transition JIRA to \"Ready for QA\"\n2. Add JIRA comment: `🐒 PR approved and merged. Moving to QA.`\n3. Clean up worktree:\n   ```bash\n   git worktree remove ../recommendations-service-RECO-XXX\n   git branch -d RECO-XXX\n   ```\n4. Update progress tracker: mark Step 14 complete, set status \"Done\"\n\n**14g. While waiting for review:**\nIf there are more tasks in the queue AND fewer than 3 tasks in review:\n→ Start the next task from **Step 2** (new plan folder, new worktree)\n→ This agent orchestrates multiple tasks, each in its own worktree\n\n---\n\n## Session Recovery\n\nIf the session dies and a new one starts:\n\n1. Check `.claude/plans/` for existing task folders\n2. Read each `progress-tracker.md` to find incomplete tasks\n3. Resume from the last unchecked step\n4. Read `context.md` and `requirements.md` for full context\n5. The worktree should still exist — verify and use it\n\nIf worktree is missing → recreate it from the existing branch.\n\n---\n\n## Safety Rails\n\n| Rule | Reason |\n|------|--------|\n| Never force-push | Destroys upstream work |\n| Never commit .env or secrets | Security |\n| Never merge PRs automatically | Humans approve and merge |\n| Max 3 concurrent tasks in review | Resource management |\n| Max 5 fix cycles per quality/test step | Prevent infinite loops |\n| Always create draft PRs | Human review required |\n| Stop on ambiguous requirements | Wrong implementation > no implementation |\n| Document all deviations in tracker | Audit trail |\n| Only orchestrator + planning agent modify tracker | Single source of truth |\n\n---\n\n## Quick Reference: Tools & Skills Used\n\n| Step | Tools / Skills |\n|------|---------------|\n| 1 | Atlassian MCP, `gh` CLI, system prompt check |\n| 2 | Write tool (create files) |\n| 3 | Atlassian MCP `searchJiraIssuesUsingJql`, AskUserQuestion |\n| 4 | Write tool |\n| 5 | Atlassian MCP `getJiraIssue`, `getTransitionsForJiraIssue` |\n| 5.5 | Read ticket data, label checks |\n| 6 | `/new-worktree` skill |\n| 7 | Agent (Opus), `/diagram-skill`, `/nestjs-skill`, `/database-skill` |\n| 7.5 | Read + analyze plan |\n| 8 | Agent (Sonnet/Opus), all CLAUDE.md rules |\n| 9 | Agent (Sonnet), `/code-quality-skill` |\n| 10 | Agent (Sonnet), `/test-skill`, test-agent patterns |\n| 11 | Agent (Sonnet), `/code-quality-skill` |\n| 12 | Agent (Sonnet), security-reviewer patterns |\n| 12.5 | Agent (Sonnet), git diff review |\n| 13 | Agent (Sonnet), `/create-pr` patterns, `gh` CLI |\n| 14 | `/loop`, Atlassian MCP, `gh` CLI, CronCreate |\n"

  "content": "# Session Recovery Guide\n\nHow to resume work when a session dies and a new one starts.\n\n## Recovery Process\n\n### 1. Detect Incomplete Work\n\n```bash\nls .claude/plans/\n```\n\nFor each folder (e.g., `RECO-555/`):\n- Read `progress-tracker.md`\n- Find the last checked step\n- If all steps checked → task is done, skip\n- If unchecked steps remain → task needs resuming\n\n### 2. Check Task Queue\n\nRead `.claude/plans/task-queue.md` (if exists):\n- Lists all tasks user requested\n- Check which ones have plan folders\n- Uncreated folders = not yet started\n\n### 3. Verify Worktrees\n\n```bash\ngit worktree list\n```\n\nFor each incomplete task:\n- Check if worktree exists at `../recommendations-service-RECO-XXX/`\n- If yes → use it\n- If no → recreate: `/new-worktree RECO-XXX`\n- Verify branch exists: `git branch --list \"RECO-XXX*\"`\n\n### 4. Check PR Status\n\nFor tasks that reached step 13+:\n```bash\ngh pr list --head RECO-XXX\ngh pr view RECO-XXX --json state,reviews,mergeable\n```\n\n- If PR exists and open → check for new comments\n- If PR merged → mark task done, clean up\n- If PR closed → investigate, may need reopening\n\n### 5. Resume from Last Step\n\nRead the progress tracker and continue from the first unchecked step.\n\n**Critical:** Read `context.md` for any deviations, blockers, or notes from the previous session.\n\n### 6. Re-establish Monitoring\n\nIf any tasks were in step 14 (PR monitoring):\n- Re-setup `/loop 10m` to poll for review comments\n- Check if new comments arrived while session was down\n\n## State Files\n\n| File | Purpose | Created |\n|------|---------|---------|\n| `progress-tracker.md` | Step-by-step status | Step 4 |\n| `requirements.md` | Ticket requirements | Step 2 |\n| `implementation-plan.md` | Architecture plan | Step 7 |\n| `context.md` | Notes, deviations, blockers | As needed |\n| `task-queue.md` | Ordered list of tasks | Step 3 (multi-task) |\n| `jira-statuses.md` | Cached JIRA transition IDs | Step 1 |\n\n## What Cannot Be Recovered\n\n- In-memory subagent state (subagents must restart from scratch)\n- `/loop` cron jobs (session-scoped, must be re-created)\n- Unsaved code changes (if worktree had uncommitted work)\n\nTo mitigate: implementation agent commits after each logical unit, not at the end.\n"

## 16 Steps (with your additions)

| # | Step | Agent | Model |
|---|------|-------|-------|
| 1 | Preflight (Atlassian + GH + auto-mode) | Orchestrator | - |
| 2 | Create plan folder `.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/` | Orchestrator | - |
| 3 | Ask user: specific tasks or pick FOR_CC from board | Orchestrator | - |
| 4 | Create progress tracker (checkbox + structured) | Orchestrator | - |
| 5 | JIRA → In Progress, read ticket deeply | Orchestrator | - |
| 5.5 | Validate ticket readiness | Orchestrator | - |
| 6 | Create worktree (`/new-worktree`) | Orchestrator | - |
| 7 | Plan implementation | Subagent | **Opus** |
| 7.5 | Orchestrator reviews plan | Orchestrator | - |
| 8 | Implement code | Subagent | Sonnet (Opus if 20+ files) |
| 9 | Code quality check #1 | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 10 | Tests: write + fix until 95% unit AND 95% e2e | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 11 | Code quality check #2 | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 12 | Security review | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 12.5 | Self-review of diff | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 13 | Create PR (multi-phase if needed, 1 at a time) | Subagent | Sonnet |
| 14 | JIRA → Code Review + `/loop 10m` monitor. Fix comments, re-test. On approval → Ready for QA, clean worktree. Start next task if queue has more. | Orchestrator + Subagents | Mixed |

**Key features:**
- Session recovery from tracker files
- Max 3 concurrent tasks in review
- Sassy preflight messages when permissions missing
- JIRA comments with monkey emoji
- All plan/context files shared across subagents
- Worktree cleanup on completion

     1→---
     2→name: ai-monkey-developer
     3→description: Autonomous AI developer agent that continuously monitors the JIRA board, picks up tickets, implements them end-to-end (plan, code, test, fix, PR), and handles PR feedback in an infinite loop. Use when user says "ai-monkey", "monkey mode", "auto-develop", "start auto mode", "autopilot", "work through the board", "pick up tickets automatically", or wants Claude to autonomously process JIRA tickets. Combines JIRA monitoring, architectural planning, implementation, testing, security review, and GitHub PR management into an autonomous development loop. Max 3 concurrent tasks (in review). Uses subagents with Sonnet for implementation and Opus for planning.
     4→---
     5→
     6→# AI Monkey Developer
     7→
     8→Autonomous infinite-loop developer. Picks up JIRA tickets, plans like a AAA architect, codes, tests, pushes PRs, handles review feedback. No breaks. No salary. No complaints.
     9→
    10→```
    11→   🐒 AI MONKEY DEVELOPER 🐒
    12→   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    13→   │  JIRA Board ──> Pick Ticket ──> Plan ──> Code ──> Test ──>    │
    14→   │       ▲         PR ──> Monitor Reviews ──> Fix ──> Approved    │
    15→   │       └────────────────── Next Ticket <────────────────────────│
    16→   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    17→```
    18→
    19→## General Instructions
    20→
    21→- **This session = orchestrator.** Never implement directly. Spawn subagents for ALL work.
    22→- **Sonnet** for all subagents by default.
    23→- **Opus** only for: planning (step 7) and complicated tasks involving 20+ files.
    24→- Every subagent MUST receive the task's plan folder as context: `.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/`
    25→- All subagents must read: `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `README.md`
    26→- Max **3 concurrent tasks** in review. If 3 are waiting for review, wait for one to finish.
    27→- Tasks from a list are processed **strictly sequentially**, never in parallel.
    28→- If session dies, a new session can resume from the tracker file.
    29→
    30→## JIRA Status Enum
    31→
    32→Fetch actual statuses from JIRA MCP on first run. Expected statuses:
    33→
    34→```
    35→TO_DO          = "To Do"
    36→IN_PROGRESS    = "In Progress"
    37→CODE_REVIEW    = "Code Review"
    38→READY_FOR_QA   = "Ready for QA"
    39→```
    40→
    41→On first run, call `getTransitionsForJiraIssue` on any RECO ticket to discover exact transition names and IDs. Save them to `.claude/plans/jira-statuses.md` for reuse.
    42→
    43→---
    44→
    45→## Workflow: 16 Steps
    46→
    47→### Step 1: Preflight Checks
    48→
    49→**Goal:** Verify all required access and permissions before starting.
    50→
    51→**1a. Atlassian MCP access:**
    52→```typescript
    53→// Call this to verify Atlassian access
    54→mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__getAccessibleAtlassianResources()
    55→```
    56→Then make a test query to the board:
    57→```typescript
    58→mcp__plugin_atlassian_atlassian__searchJiraIssuesUsingJql({
    59→  cloudId: CLOUD_ID,
    60→  jql: 'project = RECO AND status = "To Do" ORDER BY created DESC',
    61→  maxResults: 1
    62→})
    63→```
    64→If either fails → print in red:
    65→```
    66→🔴 LOGIN TO ATLASSIAN FIRST!!
    67→```
    68→Stop. Do not proceed.
    69→
    70→**1b. GitHub CLI:**
    71→```bash
    72→gh auth status
    73→```
    74→If `gh` is absent → tell user: "Install GitHub CLI: `brew install gh`"
    75→If not logged in → tell user: "Run: `gh auth login`"
    76→Stop. Do not proceed.
    77→
    78→**1c. Auto-mode check:**
    79→Check if auto-mode is active (visible in system prompt).

    84→
    85→   Try: claude --dangerously-skip-permissions

    88→Stop. Do not proceed.
    89→
    90→**Only when all three pass → proceed to Step 2.**
    91→
    92→---
    93→
    94→### Step 2: Create Plan Folder
    95→
    96→**Goal:** Create a shared workspace for all agents working on this task.
    97→
    98→```
    99→.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/
   100→├── requirements.md        ← ticket requirements, acceptance criteria, links
   101→├── implementation-plan.md ← architectural plan (created in step 7)
   102→├── progress-tracker.md    ← step-by-step progress (created in step 4)
   103→├── jira-statuses.md       ← cached JIRA transition IDs (shared across tasks)
   104→└── context.md             ← any extra context, notes, deviations

   106→
   107→Create the directory and `requirements.md` placeholder. Other files are created in later steps.
   108→
   109→If resuming a session → check if folder exists. If yes, read `progress-tracker.md` to determine where to resume.

   113→### Step 3: Ask User for Tasks
   114→
   115→**Goal:** Determine what to work on.
   116→
   117→Prompt the user:
   118→```
   119→What would you like me to work on?

   126→
   127→**Option 1 — Specific tasks:**
   128→- User provides one or more ticket keys/links
   129→- Process in the **exact order** the user listed them

   138→- Pick the first result

   236→**Goal:** Claim the ticket and deeply understand the requirements.

   242→

   246→  // Find "In Progress" transition and execute it
   247→}

   268→
   269→**Goal:** Ensure the ticket has enough info to implement.

   271→Check:

   390→
   391→**Agent working directory:** The worktree path from step 6.
   392→
   393→**Agent context:**
   394→- `.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/` (all files)
   395→- `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `README.md`
   396→
   397→**Agent instructions:**
   398→
   399→1. Read the implementation plan
   400→2. Follow the task breakdown in order
   401→3. For each file: read the most similar existing file FIRST, then match its patterns exactly
   402→4. Rules (non-negotiable):
   403→   - No comments in code (except ESLint disable)
   404→   - Named parameters (object destructured) for ALL functions
   405→   - Import aliases only (`@modules/`, `@utils/`, etc.)
   406→   - `@LogExecution` on important methods and 3rd party requests
   407→   - `knexRead` for SELECT, `knexWrite` for INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE
   408→   - Explicit return types on all functions
   409→   - `Logger` from `@nestjs/common`, never `console.log`
   410→   - Swagger decorators on all endpoints
   411→5. If new env vars needed → complete ALL 5 steps from CLAUDE.md
   412→6. Commit incrementally with `RECO-XXX: description` format
   413→
   414→---
   415→
   416→### Step 9: Code Quality Check #1
   417→
   418→**Goal:** Ensure code passes all quality gates.
   419→
   420→Spawn Sonnet subagent in the worktree:
   421→
   422→```bash
   423→npm run lint:fix
   424→npm run format
   425→npm run type-check
   426→npm run code-quality-check   # final verification
   427→```
   428→
   429→**Fix loop:** If errors remain → fix them → re-run. Max 5 cycles.
   430→If critical implementation flaws found → spawn new subagent to fix the implementation, then re-check.
   431→
   432→---
   433→
   434→### Step 10: Tests (Write + Fix + Coverage)
   435→
   436→**Goal:** 95% coverage for BOTH unit AND e2e tests independently. All tests pass.
   437→
   438→Spawn Sonnet subagent (use test-agent patterns from `.claude/agents/test-agent.md`).
   439→
   440→**Working directory:** Worktree.
   441→
   442→**Process:**
   443→
   444→1. Run existing tests first to check baseline:
   445→   ```bash
   446→   npm run test:unit:coverage
   447→   npm run test:e2e:coverage
   448→   ```
   449→
   450→2. Analyze coverage reports — identify gaps in files touched by this ticket AND project-wide
   451→
   452→3. Write new tests:
   453→   - Unit tests in `/src/unit-tests/*.unit-spec.ts`
   454→   - E2E tests in `/test/*.e2e-spec.ts`
   455→
   456→4. Fix broken tests
   457→
   458→5. Re-run coverage until:
   459→   - `npm run test:unit:coverage` → ≥95% branches/functions/lines/statements
   460→   - `npm run test:e2e:coverage` → ≥95% branches/functions/lines/statements
   461→
   462→6. All tests must pass (not just coverage)
   463→
   464→**Max 5 fix-test cycles.** If still failing → log to tracker, flag to user.
   465→
   466→---
   467→
   468→### Step 11: Code Quality Check #2
   469→
   470→**Goal:** Post-test quality verification.
   471→
   472→Spawn Sonnet subagent in worktree:
   473→
   474→```bash
   475→npm run lint
   476→npm run format
   477→npm run type-check
   478→```
   479→
   480→Fix until all three pass with zero errors.
   481→
   482→---
   483→
   484→### Step 12: Security Review
   485→
   486→**Goal:** No security vulnerabilities in the code.
   487→
   488→Spawn subagent using security-reviewer patterns (`.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md`).
   489→
   490→Check for:
   491→- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, secrets in source files
   492→- Direct `process.env` access (must use `EnvConfigService` or `getEnv()`)
   493→- Secrets logged via `@LogExecution` without `sensitiveKeys` masking
   494→- Missing `@UseGuards()` on auth-required routes
   495→- Raw SQL without parameterization in `knex.raw()`
   496→- User input in Redis keys without sanitization
   497→- Sensitive data in API responses without DTO exclusion
   498→- `.env` files or credentials staged for commit
   499→
   500→**If HIGH severity found:** Block PR creation. Spawn fix agent, then re-review.
   501→**If MEDIUM/LOW:** Log findings, proceed (note in PR description).
   502→
   503→---
   504→
   505→### Step 12.5: Self-Review
   506→
   507→**Goal:** Agent reviews its own changes like a human reviewer would.
   508→
   509→Spawn Sonnet subagent:
   510→
   511→```bash
   512→git diff main...HEAD   # or base branch
   513→```
   514→
   515→Review the diff for:
   516→- [ ] All ticket requirements addressed
   517→- [ ] No leftover debug code, TODOs, or temporary hacks
   518→- [ ] No unnecessary file changes (e.g., unrelated formatting)
   519→- [ ] Commit messages follow convention: `RECO-XXX: Imperative description`
   520→- [ ] Documentation updated if new functionality added (check `docs/` policy)
   521→- [ ] No oversized files or unnecessary complexity
   522→
   523→If issues found → fix, commit, re-review.
   524→
   525→---
   526→
   527→### Step 13: Create PR
   528→
   529→**Goal:** Create draft PR with complete, filled template.
   530→
   531→Spawn Sonnet subagent.
   532→
   533→**13a. Detect base branch:**
   534→- If branch cut from `main` → base = `main`
   535→- If stacked on another feature branch → base = that branch
   536→- Use the pattern from create-pr skill
   537→
   538→**13b. Multi-phase check:**
   539→If implementation plan says multi-phase → create PRs one at a time:
   540→```
   541→RECO-XXX [Phase 1]: Add new_col to table_name
   542→RECO-XXX [Phase 2]: Dual-write new_col, backfill rows
   543→RECO-XXX [Phase 3]: Drop old_col from table_name
   544→```
   545→Create Phase 1 PR first. Phases 2 and 3 are created after Phase 1 merges.
   546→
   547→**13c. Push and create PR:**
   548→```bash
   549→git push -u origin BRANCH_NAME
   550→
   551→gh pr create \
   552→  --draft \
   553→  --base BASE_BRANCH \
   554→  --title "RECO-XXX: Short description" \
   555→  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
   556→### Ticket 🐗
   557→JIRA task: [RECO-XXX](https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX)
   558→Design: [Figma Link](url) — omit if none
   559→
   560→### Changes 🍍
   561→- **`path/to/file.ts`** — What changed and why
   562→- **`path/to/test.unit-spec.ts`** — Test cases added
   563→
   564→### PR Checks 🐊
   565→- [x] I've self-reviewed my code.
   566→- [x] I've added the necessary labels.
   567→- [x] I've added tests for my code.
   568→- [x/blank] I've updated the documentation.
   569→
   570→### Description / Ticket Summary 🐙
   571→Business-level explanation of what and why.
   572→
   573→### Screenshots / Short Videos 🐮
   574→N/A (backend-only) or screenshots if UI changes.
   575→EOF
   576→)"
   577→```
   578→
   579→Save PR URL to progress tracker.
   580→
   581→---
   582→
   583→### Step 14: JIRA → Code Review + Monitor Loop
   584→
   585→**Goal:** Transition JIRA, monitor for human feedback, handle it, clean up.
   586→
   587→**14a. Transition JIRA to "Code Review":**
   588→```typescript
   589→// Find "Code Review" transition and execute
   590→```
   591→
   592→**14b. Add JIRA comment:**
   593→```
   594→🐒 PR created: [PR_URL]
   595→Waiting for human review.
   596→```
   597→
   598→**14c. Set up monitoring loop:**
   599→
   600→Use `/loop 10m` or CronCreate to poll every 10 minutes:
   601→
   602→```bash
   603→# Check for new review comments (from humans, not bots)
   604→PR_NUM=$(gh pr view --json number -q '.number')
   605→gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/reviews
   606→gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments
   607→
   608→# Check PR status
   609→gh pr checks $(git branch --show-current)
   610→```
   611→
   612→**14d. Handle review comments:**
   613→
   614→For each new human comment:
   615→1. Read and understand the request
   616→2. Categorize:
   617→   - Code change → spawn Sonnet agent to fix → re-run steps 9, 10, 11
   618→   - Question → reply with explanation
   619→   - Architecture concern → flag to user, don't change autonomously
   620→3. Commit + push changes
   621→4. Reply to comment:
   622→   ```bash
   623→   gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments/$COMMENT_ID/replies \
   624→     -f body="🐒 Done — [what was changed]"
   625→   ```
   626→
   627→**14e. Handle CI failures:**
   628→- Read failed check logs: `gh run view RUN_ID --log-failed`
   629→- Spawn fix agent → re-run quality + tests → push
   630→- Max 3 fix cycles
   631→
   632→**14f. On PR approval + merge-ready:**
   633→1. Transition JIRA to "Ready for QA"
   634→2. Add JIRA comment: `🐒 PR approved and merged. Moving to QA.`
   635→3. Clean up worktree:
   636→   ```bash
   637→   git worktree remove ../recommendations-service-RECO-XXX
   638→   git branch -d RECO-XXX
   639→   ```
   640→4. Update progress tracker: mark Step 14 complete, set status "Done"
   641→
   642→**14g. While waiting for review:**
   643→If there are more tasks in the queue AND fewer than 3 tasks in review:
   644→→ Start the next task from **Step 2** (new plan folder, new worktree)
   645→→ This agent orchestrates multiple tasks, each in its own worktree
   646→
   647→---
   648→
   649→## Session Recovery
   650→
   651→If the session dies and a new one starts:
   652→
   653→1. Check `.claude/plans/` for existing task folders
   654→2. Read each `progress-tracker.md` to find incomplete tasks
   655→3. Resume from the last unchecked step
   656→4. Read `context.md` and `requirements.md` for full context
   657→5. The worktree should still exist — verify and use it
   658→
   659→If worktree is missing → recreate it from the existing branch.
   660→
   661→---
   662→
   663→## Safety Rails
   664→
   665→| Rule | Reason |
   666→|------|--------|
   667→| Never force-push | Destroys upstream work |
   668→| Never commit .env or secrets | Security |
   669→| Never merge PRs automatically | Humans approve and merge |
   670→| Max 3 concurrent tasks in review | Resource management |
   671→| Max 5 fix cycles per quality/test step | Prevent infinite loops |
   672→| Always create draft PRs | Human review required |
   673→| Stop on ambiguous requirements | Wrong implementation > no implementation |
   674→| Document all deviations in tracker | Audit trail |
   675→| Only orchestrator + planning agent modify tracker | Single source of truth |
   676→
   677→---
   678→
   679→## Quick Reference: Tools & Skills Used
   680→
   681→| Step | Tools / Skills |
   682→|------|---------------|
   683→| 1 | Atlassian MCP, `gh` CLI, system prompt check |
   684→| 2 | Write tool (create files) |
   685→| 3 | Atlassian MCP `searchJiraIssuesUsingJql`, AskUserQuestion |
   686→| 4 | Write tool |
   687→| 5 | Atlassian MCP `getJiraIssue`, `getTransitionsForJiraIssue` |
   688→| 5.5 | Read ticket data, label checks |
   689→| 6 | `/new-worktree` skill |
   690→| 7 | Agent (Opus), `/diagram-skill`, `/nestjs-skill`, `/database-skill` |
   691→| 7.5 | Read + analyze plan |
   692→| 8 | Agent (Sonnet/Opus), all CLAUDE.md rules |
   693→| 9 | Agent (Sonnet), `/code-quality-skill` |
   694→| 10 | Agent (Sonnet), `/test-skill`, test-agent patterns |
   695→| 11 | Agent (Sonnet), `/code-quality-skill` |
   696→| 12 | Agent (Sonnet), security-reviewer patterns |
   697→| 12.5 | Agent (Sonnet), git diff review |
   698→| 13 | Agent (Sonnet), `/create-pr` patterns, `gh` CLI |
   699→| 14 | `/loop`, Atlassian MCP, `gh` CLI, CronCreate |
   700→
```

    17→## Common Context Block
    18→
    19→Every subagent prompt MUST include:
    20→
    21→```
    22→Working directory: {WORKTREE_PATH}
    23→Plan folder: .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/
    24→
    25→Read these files before starting:
    26→- .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/requirements.md
    27→- .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/implementation-plan.md (if exists)
    28→- .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/progress-tracker.md
    29→- CLAUDE.md
    30→- CONTRIBUTING.md
    31→- README.md
    32→```
    33→
    34→---
    35→
    36→## Planning Agent (Opus)
    37→
    38→```
    39→You are a world-class software architect (Netflix + AWS + Google + Apple level, 200 years of experience).
    40→
    41→Task: Create an implementation plan for RECO-XXX.
    42→
    43→Context:
    44→{COMMON_CONTEXT_BLOCK}
    45→
    46→Instructions:
    47→1. Read .claude/plans/RECO-XXX/requirements.md — understand every requirement
    48→2. Explore the codebase thoroughly:
    49→   - Find similar modules, services, controllers, models
    50→   - Identify existing patterns to follow
    51→   - Check src/modules/rest/ for controller patterns
    52→   - Check src/modules/db/ for model patterns
    53→   - Check src/dto/ for DTO patterns

    "timeoriginalestimate": null,
    "description": "## 🎯 Business Context / Goal\n\nThe **PDP Tabbed View Breakout Test** has concluded its evaluation phase. To reduce technical debt and maintain a streamlined Product Detail Page (PDP) architecture, we are removing the **\\[INSERT - TEST OR CONTROL\\]**\n\n## 🔧 Technical Summary\n\n* _**Feature Flag:** Retire and remove the_ `pdp_tabbed_view_breakout_test` _flag across all environments._\n* _**UI Components:** \\* Decommission the_ `ProductDetailsBreakout` _and_ `SpecificationsExpanded` _components._\n\n    * _Ensure the standard_ `TabContainer` _and_ `ProductTabPanel` _components are restored as the sole rendering method._\n    \n* _**Logic:** Remove the A/B test split logic in the_ `ProductDisplayManager`_. Revert any custom hooks (e.g.,_ `useBreakoutLayout`_) created specifically for this experiment._\n* _**Styles:** Purge the_ `pdp-breakout.module.scss` _and remove any unique grid layouts defined in the global layout CSS used only for this test._\n\n## ✅ Acceptance Criteria / Rendering Conditions\n\n## 📁 Repository List\n\n* `catalog`\n\n## 🚦 Release Timeline & Expectations\n\n* **Target Release Date:** 2026-02-02 - following the wrap up of test\n\n## 🧪 QA / Testing Requirements\n\n* **Regression:** Verify that the \"Add to Cart\" and \"Size Selector\" remain fully functional and aligned now that the layout is back to baseline.\n* **Performance:** Run a Lighthouse audit on a standard PDP to ensure the removal of the experiment logic has slightly improved TBT (Total Blocking Time).\n* **Validation:** Confirm no 404s for deleted CSS or JS assets in the browser console.\n\n",
    "customfield_12554": {

      "version": 1,

        "id": 8763,
        "name": "RECO 4",
        "state": "closed",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch March Promo (Office Supplies, Tech, Awards) — Build out endpoint and UI to support upcoming promo, enabling targeted product discovery for these categories\n2. Email Abandon Cart Testing — Launch A/B test adding trending products and pricing to the new user abandon cart experience\n3. Improve Favorites Experience — Deliver the No Favorites Selected empty state to improve onboarding UX for new users\n4. Bug Fix & Code Cleanup — Suppress out-of-stock items from \"More from this Brand\" recommendations and complete Sweats Promo code cleanup",
        "startDate": "2026-02-25T13:15:28.155Z",
        "endDate": "2026-03-11T13:15:11.000Z",
        "completeDate": "2026-03-11T12:28:15.876Z"
      },
      {
        "id": 7700,
        "name": "RECO 2",
        "state": "closed",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch FBT Recos in Cart: Enable \"Frequently Bought Together\" recommendations to drive cross-sells by 2/4.\n2. Sweats Promo Readiness: Implement guided discovery for trending products to streamline the sale UX.\n3. Sweats Promo A/B Test: Prepare a \"Pinned Product\" test to highlight top-tier quality and price points.\n4. RFC - Reco Widget: Complete and submit the Request for Comments for the new recommendation widget.",
        "startDate": "2026-01-28T13:11:17.985Z",
        "endDate": "2026-02-11T13:11:00.000Z",
        "completeDate": "2026-02-11T13:23:38.806Z"
      },
      {
        "id": 7733,
        "name": "RECO 3",
        "state": "closed",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch Add Products Lab UI for compatible styles to allow users an easier process for adding products to their cart\n2. Launch Trending Products - Promo Products - V2 A/B Test to allow more data into Trending model to better upgrade our product recommendations\n3. Train Frequently Bought Together V2 - Begin research into incorporating next data set to better improve FBT model\n4. Migrate Reco widget to Pigment to begin necessary work to create repeatable widget across the site",
        "startDate": "2026-02-11T13:25:42.838Z",
        "endDate": "2026-02-25T13:25:28.000Z",
        "completeDate": "2026-02-25T13:13:21.016Z"
      },
      {
        "id": 10126,
        "name": "RECO 6",
        "state": "active",
        "boardId": 777,
        "goal": "1. Launch & Transition April Promo: Turn off the March Promo and fully ship the April Promo for Drinkware & Bags.\n\n2. Migrate PDP Recommendations Widget to NFE: Complete the PDP widget migration to Next Frontend, enabling faster iteration going forward.\n\n3. Lay the Foundation for Data-Driven Experimentation: Implement product position tracking for recommendation and promotion events to power future A/B analysis.\n\n4. Advance Key A/B Tests, Platform Integrations & Design Foundation: Complete the Color Swatches endpoint, kick off the A/B test pending UX designs, progress the CMS integration, and deliver the canonical Reco Figma space.\n\n5. Maintain Codebase Health & NFE Parity: Clean up March Promo and Breakout test code, and deliver NFE Catalog parity for Budget Friendly carousel and the 4→5 card A/B test.",
        "startDate": "2026-03-25T12:25:16.208Z",
        "endDate": "2026-04-08T12:28:54.000Z"
      }
    ],

    "description": "## 🎯 Business Context / Goal\n\nThe **PDP Tabbed View Breakout Test** has concluded its evaluation phase. To reduce technical debt and maintain a streamlined Product Detail Page (PDP) architecture, we are removing the **\\[INSERT - TEST OR CONTROL\\]**\n\n## 🔧 Technical Summary\n\n* _**Feature Flag:** Retire and remove the_ `pdp_tabbed_view_breakout_test` _flag across all environments._\n* _**UI Components:** \\* Decommission the_ `ProductDetailsBreakout` _and_ `SpecificationsExpanded` _components._\n\n    * _Ensure the standard_ `TabContainer` _and_ `ProductTabPanel` _components are restored as the sole rendering method._\n    \n* _**Logic:** Remove the A/B test split logic in the_ `ProductDisplayManager`_. Revert any custom hooks (e.g.,_ `useBreakoutLayout`_) created specifically for this experiment._\n* _**Styles:** Purge the_ `pdp-breakout.module.scss` _and remove any unique grid layouts defined in the global layout CSS used only for this test._\n\n## ✅ Acceptance Criteria / Rendering Conditions\n\n## 📁 Repository List\n\n* `catalog`\n\n## 🚦 Release Timeline & Expectations\n\n* **Target Release Date:** 2026-02-02 - following the wrap up of test\n\n## 🧪 QA / Testing Requirements\n\n* **Regression:** Verify that the \"Add to Cart\" and \"Size Selector\" remain fully functional and aligned now that the layout is back to baseline.\n* **Performance:** Run a Lighthouse audit on a standard PDP to ensure the removal of the experiment logic has slightly improved TBT (Total Blocking Time).\n* **Validation:** Confirm no 404s for deleted CSS or JS assets in the browser console.\n\n",
    "customfield_12554": {

      "version": 1,

- Tasks from a list are processed **strictly sequentially**, never in parallel.
- If session dies, a new session can resume from the tracker file.

   771→

   774→| Never force-push                                  | Destroys upstream work                   |
   775→| Never commit .env or secrets                      | Security                                 |

Excellent! I've now gathered all the skill files. Let me create a comprehensive summary report.

## Summary: Complete Superpowers Plugin Skills Content

I have successfully located and read ALL 14 skill files from the superpowers plugin (v5.0.6) in `~/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/superpowers/5.0.6/skills/`. Here's the complete compilation:

### Skill Files Read (14 total)

All skills are located in: `/Users/4thwithme/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/superpowers/5.0.6/skills/`

1. **using-git-worktrees/SKILL.md** - Create isolated workspaces sharing the same repository. Covers directory selection (priority: existing > CLAUDE.md > ask user), safety verification (must check .gitignore), creation steps, setup automation, and baseline testing.

2. **test-driven-development/SKILL.md** - Write test first, watch it fail, write minimal code to pass. The Iron Law: "NO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST". Covers red-green-refactor cycle, common rationalizations, and verification checklist.

3. **systematic-debugging/SKILL.md** - ALWAYS find root cause before attempting fixes. Four phases: Root Cause Investigation, Pattern Analysis, Hypothesis and Testing, Implementation. Includes multi-component diagnostics and root-cause-tracing techniques.

4. **using-superpowers/SKILL.md** - Master skill: invoke relevant skills BEFORE any response (even 1% chance applies). Instruction priority: User > Skills > Default. Covers skill invocation workflow and red flags for rationalization.

5. **dispatching-parallel-agents/SKILL.md** - Delegate 2+ independent tasks to specialized agents in parallel. Covers when to use (3+ failures, independent domains), agent prompt structure (focused, self-contained, specific output), and real-world impact.

6. **executing-plans/SKILL.md** - Load plan, review critically, execute all tasks, report completion. Covers three steps: Load and Review Plan, Execute Tasks (mark in_progress per item, follow steps exactly, verify), Complete Development (use finishing-a-development-branch skill).

7. **finishing-a-development-branch/SKILL.md** - Verify tests → Present 4 options (Merge locally, Push & PR, Keep as-is, Discard) → Execute choice → Cleanup worktree. Requires test verification before offering options, typed confirmation for discard.

8. **brainstorming/SKILL.md** - Turn ideas into designs through collaborative dialogue. 9-step checklist: explore context, offer visual companion (if needed), ask clarifying questions one at a time, propose 2-3 approaches, present design sections, write design doc, spec self-review, user reviews spec, invoke writing-plans skill.

9. **writing-plans/SKILL.md** - Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming zero context. Covers file structure mapping, bite-sized task granularity (2-5 min each), task structure with checkbox syntax, no placeholders rule, self-review (spec coverage, placeholder scan, type consistency), execution handoff (subagent-driven vs. inline).

10. **requesting-code-review/SKILL.md** - Dispatch code-reviewer subagent for production readiness. Covers when to request (mandatory after each task in subagent-driven, before merge; optional when stuck), how to request (get git SHAs, dispatch with template), and acting on feedback.

11. **receiving-code-review/SKILL.md** - Technical evaluation, not emotional performance. Forbidden: performative agreement ("You're absolutely right!"). Required: Restate requirement, ask clarifications, verify against codebase, push back with technical reasoning, implement one at a time.

12. **writing-skills/SKILL.md** - Writing skills IS TDD for process documentation. The Iron Law: "NO SKILL WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST". Covers skill types (Technique, Pattern, Reference), CSO (Claude Search Optimization - description = when to use, NOT what skill does), flowchart usage, bulletproofing against rationalization.

13. **verification-before-completion/SKILL.md** - Evidence before claims, always. Gate Function: IDENTIFY command → RUN → READ output → VERIFY → THEN claim. Common failures table shows claims that require specific verification (tests pass, linter clean, bug fixed, etc.).

14. **subagent-driven-development/SKILL.md** - Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review (spec compliance first, then code quality). Covers when to use, process flow (with detailed flowchart), model selection guidance, handling implementer status (DONE, DONE_WITH_CONCERNS, NEEDS_CONTEXT, BLOCKED).

### Supporting Files

**Agent Definition:**
- `/agents/code-reviewer.md` - Senior Code Reviewer agent for reviewing completed work against plans. Covers plan alignment, code quality, architecture, documentation, issue categorization (Critical/Important/Suggestions).

**Subagent Prompt Templates:**
- `requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md` - Template for dispatching code reviewers with review checklist
- `subagent-driven-development/implementer-prompt.md` - Template for dispatching implementer subagents (2-5 min work chunks)
- `subagent-driven-development/spec-reviewer-prompt.md` - Template for spec compliance review (verify built what was requested)
- `subagent-driven-development/code-quality-reviewer-prompt.md` - Template for code quality review (after spec compliance passes)

**Commands (Deprecated):**
- `commands/execute-plan.md` - Deprecated (use executing-plans skill)
- `commands/write-plan.md` - Deprecated (use writing-plans skill)
- `commands/brainstorm.md` - Deprecated (use brainstorming skill)

### Key Cross-Skill Dependencies

**Workflow Chain:**
brainstorming → writing-plans → (choose execution) → {subagent-driven-development OR executing-plans} → finishing-a-development-branch

**Required Sub-Skills by Skill:**
- brainstorming: REQUIRES writing-plans (next step only)
- writing-plans: REQUIRES brainstorming (previous context)
- executing-plans: REQUIRES using-git-worktrees, finishing-a-development-branch
- subagent-driven-development: REQUIRES using-git-worktrees, requesting-code-review, finishing-a-development-branch
- systematic-debugging: REQUIRES test-driven-development (for Phase 4)
- writing-skills: REQUIRES test-driven-development (background)

**Discipline-Enforcing Rules:**
- test-driven-development: Iron Law - NO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT FAILING TEST FIRST
- verification-before-completion: Iron Law - NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
- systematic-debugging: Iron Law - NO FIXES WITHOUT ROOT CAUSE INVESTIGATION FIRST
- writing-skills: Iron Law - NO SKILL WITHOUT FAILING TEST FIRST (TDD for documentation)

All files have been fully read and are available at the absolute paths listed above.

Done. Superpowers fully integrated into both SKILL.md and subagent-prompts.md.

**Summary of superpowers integration:**

| Superpowers Skill | Where Used | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `writing-plans` | Step 7 (Planning) | Zero-context plans, 2-5 min tasks, no placeholders |
| `brainstorming` | Step 7 (if vague reqs) | Collaborative design before planning |
| `executing-plans` | Step 8 (Implementation) | Load plan, mark in_progress, verify each task |
| `subagent-driven-development` | Steps 8-10 | Fresh agent per task, two-stage review |
| `dispatching-parallel-agents` | Steps 9+10 | Parallel quality + tests when independent |
| `test-driven-development` | Step 10 (Tests) | Iron Law: no code without failing test |
| `systematic-debugging` | Any failure | Root cause before fix |
| `verification-before-completion` | Steps 9, 11, 12.1 | Run → Read → THEN claim |
| `using-git-worktrees` | Step 6 | Safety verification |
| `requesting-code-review` | Step 12.1 | Dispatch `code-reviewer` agent |
| `receiving-code-review` | Step 14 | No performative agreement, push back with reasoning |
| `finishing-a-development-branch` | Step 13 | Verify → Push → PR → Cleanup |

**3 Iron Laws enforced:**
1. NO production code without failing test first
2. NO completion claims without fresh verification evidence
3. NO fixes without root cause investigation first

     1→---
     2→name: ai-monkey-developer
     3→description: Autonomous AI developer agent that continuously monitors the JIRA board, picks up tickets, implements them end-to-end (plan, code, test, fix, PR), and handles PR feedback in an infinite loop. Use when user says "ai-monkey", "monkey mode", "auto-develop", "start auto mode", "autopilot", "work through the board", "pick up tickets automatically", or wants Claude to autonomously process JIRA tickets.      4→---
     5→
     6→# AI Monkey Developer
     7→
     8→Autonomous infinite-loop developer. Picks up JIRA tickets, plans like a AAA architect, codes, tests, pushes PRs, handles review feedback. No breaks. No salary. No complaints.
     9→
    10→## Workflow Overview
    11→
    12→```
    13→   🐒 AI MONKEY DEVELOPER 🐒
    14→   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    15→   │  JIRA Board ──> Pick Ticket ──> Plan ──> Code ──> Test ──>    │
    16→   │       ▲         PR ──> Monitor Reviews ──> Fix ──> Approved    │
    17→   │       └────────────────── Next Ticket <────────────────────────│
    18→   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    19→```
    20→
    21→## JIRA Configuration
    22→
    23→**Board:** https://customink.atlassian.net/jira/software/c/projects/RECO/boards/777
    24→**Project:** RECO
    25→**Cloud ID:** `1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380` (customink.atlassian.net)
    26→**Auth:** Atlassian MCP plugin with browser OAuth (no API token needed)
    27→
    28→### Label Field for Auto-Pick
    29→
    30→The FOR_CC label lives in the standard JIRA `labels` field (array of strings).
    31→JQL filter: `labels = "FOR_CC"`
    32→API response path: `issue.fields.labels` → `["FOR_CC", ...]`
    33→
    34→### JIRA Status Enum (verified 2026-03-25)
    35→
    36→All transitions are **global** — available from any status. Use transition ID to move tickets.
    37→
    38→```
    39→| Status           | Status ID | Transition ID | Category     |
    40→|------------------|-----------|---------------|--------------|
    41→| To Do            | 10436     | 11            | To Do        |
    42→| In Progress      | 3         | 21            | In Progress  |
    43→| Code Complete    | 12985     | 41            | In Progress  |
    44→| Ready for QA     | 11524     | 51            | In Progress  |
    45→| QA               | 12400     | 61            | In Progress  |
    46→| Ready for Release| 12986     | 71            | In Progress  |
    47→| Testing          | 10020     | 81            | In Progress  |
    48→| Done             | 10019     | 31            | Done         |
    49→```
    50→
    51→**AI Monkey Developer uses these transitions:**
    52→
    53→```
    54→PICK_UP_TICKET:   transition ID 21  → "In Progress"
    55→PR_CREATED:       transition ID 41  → "Code Complete"
    56→READY_FOR_QA:     transition ID 51  → "Ready for QA"
    57→```
    58→
    59→**Note:** There is NO "Code Review" status. The closest is **"Code Complete"** (ID 12985, transition 41). Use this when PR is created and waiting for review.
    60→
    61→**To transition a ticket:**
    62→
    63→```typescript
    64→mcp__atlassian__transitionJiraIssue({
    65→	cloudId: '1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380',
    66→	issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-XXX',
    67→	transition: { id: '21' }, // e.g. "In Progress"
    68→});
    69→```
    70→

    73→## Workflow: 16 Steps
    74→
    75→### Step 1: Preflight Checks
    76→
    77→**Goal:** Verify all required access and permissions before starting.
    78→
    79→**1a. Atlassian MCP access:**
    80→
    81→```typescript
    82→// Verify access — uses browser OAuth, no API token needed
    83→mcp__atlassian__getAccessibleAtlassianResources();
    84→```
    85→
    86→Then test query to the board:
    87→
    88→```typescript
    89→mcp__atlassian__searchJiraIssuesUsingJql({

    91→	jql: 'project = RECO AND status = "To Do" ORDER BY created DESC',
    92→	maxResults: 1,
    93→});
    94→```
    95→
    96→If either fails → print in red:
    97→

   101→```

   103→Stop. Do not proceed.

   112→If not logged in → tell user: "Run: `gh auth login`"
   113→Stop. Do not proceed.

   134→
   135→**Goal:** Create a shared workspace for all agents working on this task.

   152→### Step 3: Ask User for Tasks
   153→
   154→**Goal:** Determine what to work on.
   155→
   156→Prompt the user:
   157→
   158→```
   159→What would you like me to work on?
   160→
   161→1. Specific task(s) — paste JIRA link(s) or ticket key(s)
   162→2. Pick from board — I'll find "To Do" tickets labeled FOR_CC
   163→
   164→Your choice:
   165→```
   166→
   167→**Option 1 — Specific tasks:**
   168→
   169→- User provides one or more ticket keys/links
   170→- Process in the **exact order** the user listed them
   171→- Save the ordered list to `.claude/plans/task-queue.md`
   172→
   173→**Option 2 — Pick from board:**
   174→Board: https://customink.atlassian.net/jira/software/c/projects/RECO/boards/777
   175→
   176→Query JIRA using `labels` field (standard JIRA field, array of strings):
   177→
   178→```typescript
   179→mcp__atlassian__searchJiraIssuesUsingJql({
   180→	cloudId: '1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380',
   181→	jql: 'project = RECO AND status = "To Do" AND labels = "FOR_CC" AND sprint in openSprints() ORDER BY priority DESC, created ASC',
   182→	maxResults: 5,
   183→});
   184→```
   185→
   186→Verify response: `issue.fields.labels` should contain `"FOR_CC"`.
   187→
   188→- Pick the first result
   189→- If no tickets found → tell user "No FOR_CC tickets in To Do. Board is clear. Check: https://customink.atlassian.net/jira/software/c/projects/RECO/boards/777"
   190→
   191→**For the selected ticket:**
   192→
   193→- Save all requirements, acceptance criteria, description, and key info to `.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/requirements.md`
   194→- If ticket has links (Figma, Confluence, etc.) → try to open and analyze them, save relevant info to requirements.md

   286→### Step 5: Set JIRA Status + Read Ticket
   287→
   288→**Goal:** Claim the ticket and deeply understand the requirements.
   289→
   290→**5a. Transition to "In Progress":**
   291→
   292→```typescript
   293→// Get current status

   296→	issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-XXX',
   297→});
   298→
   299→// Only transition if "To Do" (status ID 10436)
   300→if (task.fields.status.id === '10436') {
   301→	await transitionJiraIssue({
   302→		cloudId: '1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380',
   303→		issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-XXX',

   458→
   459→**Goal:** Write the code following all project conventions.

   508→

   542→### Step 11: Code Quality Check #2
   543→
   544→**Goal:** Post-test quality verification.
   545→
   546→Spawn Sonnet subagent in worktree:
   547→
   548→```bash

   557→
   558→### Step 12: Security Review
   559→
   560→**Goal:** No security vulnerabilities in the code.
   561→
   562→Spawn subagent using security-reviewer patterns (`.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md`).
   563→
   564→Check for:
   565→
   566→- Hardcoded API keys, tokens, secrets in source files
   567→- Direct `process.env` access (must use `EnvConfigService` or `getEnv()`)
   568→- Secrets logged via `@LogExecution` without `sensitiveKeys` masking
   569→- Missing `@UseGuards()` on auth-required routes
   570→- Raw SQL without parameterization in `knex.raw()`
   571→- User input in Redis keys without sanitization
   572→- Sensitive data in API responses without DTO exclusion
   573→- `.env` files or credentials staged for commit
   574→
   575→**If HIGH severity found:** Block PR creation. Spawn fix agent, then re-review.
   576→**If MEDIUM/LOW:** Log findings, proceed (note in PR description).
   577→

   603→### Step 13: Create PR
   604→
   605→**Goal:** Create draft PR with complete, filled template.
   606→
   607→Spawn Sonnet subagent.
   608→
   609→**13a. Detect base branch:**
   610→
   611→- If branch cut from `main` → base = `main`
   612→- If stacked on another feature branch → base = that branch
   613→- Use the pattern from create-pr skill
   614→
   615→**13b. Multi-phase check:**
   616→If implementation plan says multi-phase → create PRs one at a time:
   617→
   618→```
   619→RECO-XXX [Phase 1]: Add new_col to table_name
   620→RECO-XXX [Phase 2]: Dual-write new_col, backfill rows
   621→RECO-XXX [Phase 3]: Drop old_col from table_name
   622→```
   623→
   624→Create Phase 1 PR first. Phases 2 and 3 are created after Phase 1 merges.
   625→
   626→**13c. Push and create PR:**
   627→
   628→```bash
   629→git push -u origin BRANCH_NAME
   630→
   631→gh pr create \
   632→  --draft \
   633→  --base BASE_BRANCH \
   634→  --title "RECO-XXX: Short description" \
   635→  --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
   636→### Ticket 🐗
   637→JIRA task: [RECO-XXX](https://customink.atlassian.net/browse/RECO-XXX)
   638→Design: [Figma Link](url) — omit if none
   639→
   640→### Changes 🍍
   641→- **`path/to/file.ts`** — What changed and why
   642→- **`path/to/test.unit-spec.ts`** — Test cases added
   643→
   644→### PR Checks 🐊
   645→- [x] I've self-reviewed my code.
   646→- [x] I've added the necessary labels.
   647→- [x] I've added tests for my code.
   648→- [x/blank] I've updated the documentation.
   649→
   650→### Description / Ticket Summary 🐙
   651→Business-level explanation of what and why.
   652→
   653→### Screenshots / Short Videos 🐮
   654→N/A (backend-only) or screenshots if UI changes.
   655→EOF
   656→)"
   657→```
   658→
   659→Save PR URL to progress tracker.
   660→
   661→---
   662→
   663→### Step 14: JIRA → Code Complete + Monitor Loop
   664→
   665→**Goal:** Transition JIRA, monitor for human feedback, handle it, clean up.
   666→
   667→**14a. Transition JIRA to "Code Complete":**
   668→
   669→```typescript
   670→mcp__atlassian__transitionJiraIssue({
   671→	cloudId: '1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380',
   672→	issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-XXX',
   673→	transition: { id: '41' }, // → "Code Complete"
   674→});
   675→```
   676→
   677→**14b. Add JIRA comment:**
   678→
   679→```
   680→🐒 PR created: [PR_URL]
   681→Waiting for human review.
   682→```
   683→
   684→**14c. Set up monitoring loop:**
   685→
   686→Use `/loop 10m` or CronCreate to poll every 10 minutes:
   687→
   688→```bash
   689→# Check for new review comments (from humans, not bots)
   690→PR_NUM=$(gh pr view --json number -q '.number')
   691→gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/reviews
   692→gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments
   693→
   694→# Check PR status
   695→gh pr checks $(git branch --show-current)
   696→```
   697→
   698→**14d. Handle review comments:**
   699→
   700→For each new human comment:
   701→
   702→1. Read and understand the request
   703→2. Categorize:
   704→   - Code change → spawn Sonnet agent to fix → re-run steps 9, 10, 11
   705→   - Question → reply with explanation
   706→   - Architecture concern → flag to user, don't change autonomously
   707→3. Commit + push changes
   708→4. Reply to comment:
   709→   ```bash
   710→   gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$PR_NUM/comments/$COMMENT_ID/replies \
   711→     -f body="🐒 Done — [what was changed]"
   712→   ```
   713→
   714→**14e. Handle CI failures:**
   715→
   716→- Read failed check logs: `gh run view RUN_ID --log-failed`
   717→- Spawn fix agent → re-run quality + tests → push
   718→- Max 3 fix cycles
   719→
   720→**14f. On PR approval + merge-ready:**
   721→
   722→1. Transition JIRA to "Ready for QA":
   723→   ```typescript
   724→   mcp__atlassian__transitionJiraIssue({
   725→   	cloudId: '1ad24543-cd69-4ee1-82fc-d6a43602b380',
   726→   	issueIdOrKey: 'RECO-XXX',
   727→   	transition: { id: '51' }, // → "Ready for QA"
   728→   });
   729→   ```
   730→2. Add JIRA comment: `🐒 PR approved and merged. Moving to QA.`
   731→3. Clean up worktree:
   732→   ```bash
   733→   git worktree remove ../recommendations-service-RECO-XXX
   734→   git branch -d RECO-XXX
   735→   ```
   736→4. Update progress tracker: mark Step 14 complete, set status "Done"
   737→
   738→**14g. While waiting for review:**
   739→If there are more tasks in the queue AND fewer than 3 tasks in review:
   740→→ Start the next task from **Step 2** (new plan folder, new worktree)
   741→→ This agent orchestrates multiple tasks, each in its own worktree
   742→
   743→---
   744→
   745→## Session Recovery
   746→
   747→If the session dies and a new one starts:
   748→
   749→1. Check `.claude/plans/` for existing task folders
   750→2. Read each `progress-tracker.md` to find incomplete tasks
   751→3. Resume from the last unchecked step
   752→4. Read `context.md` and `requirements.md` for full context
   753→5. The worktree should still exist — verify and use it
   754→
   755→If worktree is missing → recreate it from the existing branch.
   756→
   757→---
   758→
   759→## Safety Rails
   760→
   761→| Rule                                              | Reason                                   |
   762→| ------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
   763→| Never force-push                                  | Destroys upstream work                   |
   764→| Never commit .env or secrets                      | Security                                 |
   765→| Never merge PRs automatically                     | Humans approve and merge                 |
   766→| Max 3 concurrent tasks in review                  | Resource management                      |
   767→| Max 5 fix cycles per quality/test step            | Prevent infinite loops                   |
   768→| Always create draft PRs                           | Human review required                    |
   769→| Stop on ambiguous requirements                    | Wrong implementation > no implementation |
   770→| Document all deviations in tracker                | Audit trail                              |
   771→| Only orchestrator + planning agent modify tracker | Single source of truth                   |
   772→
   773→---
   774→
   775→## Quick Reference: Tools, Skills & General Instructions
   776→
   777→### Orchestrator Rules
   778→
   779→- **This session = orchestrator.** Never implement directly. Spawn subagents for ALL work.
   780→- **Sonnet** for all subagents by default.
   781→- **Opus** only for: planning (step 7) and complicated tasks involving 20+ files.
   782→- Every subagent MUST receive the task's plan folder as context: `.claude/plans/RECO-XXX/`
   783→- All subagents must read: `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `README.md`
   784→- Max **3 concurrent tasks** in review. If 3 are waiting for review, wait for one to finish.
   785→- Tasks from a list are processed **strictly sequentially**, never in parallel.
   786→- If session dies, a new session can resume from the tracker file.
   787→- Use `dispatching-parallel-agents` pattern when 2+ independent tasks exist within a step.
   788→- Use `subagent-driven-development` pattern: fresh subagent per task + two-stage review.
   789→
   790→### Superpowers Integration
   791→
   792→The superpowers plugin provides discipline-enforcing skills. Apply them at these steps:
   793→
   794→| Superpowers Skill                | When to Use                                                      |
   795→| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
   796→| `writing-plans`                  | Step 7 — plan must be zero-context, bite-sized tasks (2-5 min)   |
   797→| `executing-plans`                | Step 8 — load plan, mark tasks in_progress, verify each          |
   798→| `subagent-driven-development`    | Steps 8-10 — fresh subagent per task, two-stage review           |
   799→| `dispatching-parallel-agents`    | Steps 9+10 — run quality + tests in parallel when independent    |
   800→| `test-driven-development`        | Step 10 — NO production code without failing test first           |
   801→| `systematic-debugging`           | Any failure — find root cause BEFORE attempting fix               |
   802→| `verification-before-completion` | Steps 9, 11, 12.1 — run command, read output, THEN claim pass    |
   803→| `using-git-worktrees`            | Step 6 — worktree creation and safety verification               |
   804→| `requesting-code-review`         | Step 12.1 — dispatch `code-reviewer` agent for self-review       |
   805→| `receiving-code-review`          | Step 14 — handle human review without performative agreement      |
   806→| `finishing-a-development-branch` | Step 13 — verify tests → push & PR → cleanup                     |
   807→| `brainstorming`                  | Step 7 (if requirements are vague) — collaborative design first   |
   808→
   809→**Iron Laws (from superpowers — enforce always):**
   810→- `test-driven-development`: NO production code without a failing test first
   811→- `verification-before-completion`: NO completion claims without fresh verification evidence
   812→- `systematic-debugging`: NO fixes without root cause investigation first
   813→

   236→- E2E tests: test complete API workflows

   247→Security review for RECO-XXX implementation.
   248→
   249→Working directory: {WORKTREE_PATH}

   251→SKILLS & TOOLS TO USE EXPLICITLY:
   252→- Reference .claude/agents/security-reviewer.md for security review patterns and checklist
   253→- Use Agent tool with subagent_type="security-reviewer" for deep security analysis
   254→- Use Grep tool to search for patterns: process.env, knex.raw, hardcoded strings, .env references
   255→
   256→Review ALL changed files (git diff main...HEAD) for:
   257→
   258→1. Secrets & env vars:
   259→   - Hardcoded API keys, tokens, secrets
   260→   - Direct process.env access (must use EnvConfigService or getEnv())
   261→   - Secrets logged via @LogExecution without sensitiveKeys masking
   262→
   263→2. Auth & access control:
   264→   - Missing @UseGuards() on auth-required routes
   265→   - Auth bypass possibilities
   266→   - Missing rate limiting (@Throttle) on public routes
   267→
   268→3. Injection risks:
   269→   - Raw SQL in knex.raw() without parameterization
   270→   - Unsanitized user input in Algolia queries
   271→   - SQS messages without schema validation
   272→
   273→4. Data exposure:
   274→   - Sensitive fields in API responses without DTO exclusion
   275→   - @LogExecution logging sensitive response data
   276→   - .env files or credentials in git staging area
   277→
   278→5. Redis & SQS:
   279→   - Redis keys from user input without sanitization
   280→   - Missing TTL on sensitive cache entries
   281→   - Missing idempotency checks on SQS processing
   282→
   283→Output format:
   284→SEVERITY: HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW
   285→FILE: path/to/file.ts:line
   286→ISSUE: Description
   287→FIX: Specific remediation
   288→
   289→If HIGH severity found → these MUST be fixed before PR creation.
   290→If MEDIUM/LOW → note in findings, proceed.
   291→```

   295→## Self-Review Agent (Sonnet) — Step 12.1
   296→

   325→Output: List of issues found, or "Clean — ready for PR".

   338→Context:
